# 05 · Plan and status

The project's memory. Claude reads this at the start of every task and writes to it at the end of every task, so a new session picks up exactly where the last one stopped.

**Last updated:** 2026-08-22

---

## Where we are right now

**Phase 1 is complete.** **Phase 2 is underway:** nav and Hero are built, both from the real Figma design, both for desktop and mobile together. `/` now renders both, with twelve sections still to come.

**Next up:** the rest of Phase 2. Recommended model: **Sonnet 5 at high effort** — these compose components that already exist, so the hard design calls are behind us.

## Phase 0 · Foundations — Complete

Next.js App Router with TypeScript and Tailwind v4. Figtree via `next/font`. Design tokens encoded in the `@theme` block of `app/globals.css`, which is both the CSS variables and the Tailwind utilities, so they cannot drift. Playwright screenshot QA across all fifteen target viewports. All copy moved into `content/home.ts`.

## Phase 1 · Design system and components — Complete

### 1a · Tokens and styleguide
Every colour, type size, spacing step, radius, shadow and layout rule rendered live at `/styleguide` from the theme itself. Heading sizes fit as a single fluid curve through the exact 360px and 1440px anchors, continuing at the same rate to 1920px and then holding flat.

### 1b · First five components
`Button`, `SectionHeading`, `Card` (plus `CapabilityCard`), `StatBlock`, `FeatureNumbered`.

### 1c · Remaining components — 2026-08-21
`Eyebrow`, `Chip`, `MediaPlaceholder`, `LogoRow`, `Marquee`, `Accordion`, `Header`, `MobileNav`. Only the `Footer` remains unbuilt, deliberately.

Verified: 15 viewports with no horizontal overflow; mega menu opens and closes on Escape; drawer traps focus, locks scroll, and returns focus to the menu button.

Two things surfaced during the build and were fixed: the drawer had to be moved onto a portal, because the sticky header's layer would otherwise trap it beneath other page content; and the media placeholder's label collided with the hero play button, so it now drops to the bottom when an overlay is present.

### 1d · Working agreement, knowledge base, style library — 2026-08-21
Set the baseline for how the project runs from here.

- **Knowledge base:** `CLAUDE.md` slimmed to rules-and-index; the detail split into the five numbered docs in `/docs`. Raw source material moved to `docs/source/` and the wireframe filenames fixed.
- **Style library:** new `components/ui/styles.ts` holding every class string that describes appearance, plus a `cx()` helper replacing the join pattern that had been copy-pasted into eight components. All thirteen components retrofitted onto it, so no component contains a styling decision any more.
- **Verified identical:** a pixel baseline of `/styleguide` at 390, 768 and 1440 was captured before the retrofit and compared after, with a zero-pixel-difference tolerance. All three matched exactly. Build, types, lint and the 15-viewport suite all clean.

### 1e · Typography adopted from Figma exactly — 2026-08-22
Replaced the entire type scale with the thirteen text styles from the Figma file "Caprio Website" (node 284:333): sizes, weights, line heights and letter-spacing (all zero) copied exactly, by explicit owner request. This is the one deliberate exception to "Figma is layout only" — colour, spacing and radius still follow our own tokens, and Figma's differ there too (see decision log).

Five component-to-style pairings came from Figma's own components rather than judgement, and three would have been wrong guessed: `SectionHeading` pairs Overline with **Heading 1** (not Heading 2 — Figma's "Section Header"), `StatBlock` uses **Heading 1** for its value (not the Stat Number style — Figma's "Stat Card"), `CapabilityCard`/`FeatureNumbered` use Heading 3 + Body Large ("Feature Card"), `Accordion` uses Heading 5 + Body ("FAQ Item"), `Button` uses the Button style. Everything without a Figma component (chips, nav links, ticker items) moved to Button Small, the smallest style in the scale, once the old 14px size was retired — a judgement call, not a Figma fact.

**Consequence surfaced and resolved with the owner:** at Figma's real sizes, the header's nav row does not fit in one line below roughly 1420px wide — past both of Tailwind's `lg` and `xl` breakpoints, short of `2xl`. Two required desktop viewports, 1280×720 and 1366×768, are narrower than that. Rather than guess, this was put to the owner directly: the desktop nav now appears at a custom 1420px breakpoint; those two viewports show the drawer, and 1440×900 / 1920×1080 show the full nav. Documented in [03-component-library.md](03-component-library.md#header--built).

**Verified:** new permanent test `tests/typography.spec.ts` asserts all thirteen tokens against the Figma table at 1440px, plus that fixed styles hold on mobile and nothing drops under 14px — 15 assertions, all passing. The 15-viewport overflow suite passes. The nav interaction check (mega menu Escape, drawer focus trap, focus return) re-run and passing at the new breakpoint. Build, types and lint clean. Grep confirms no `text-small` or `text-eyebrow` remains anywhere.

## Phase 2 · Homepage — In progress

Fourteen sections, in wireframe order, composed from the existing components. The mapping of section to components is in [03-component-library.md](03-component-library.md).

### 2a · Nav — 2026-08-22
Built the real nav from the actual Figma design (node `316:1331`) and the real CAPRIO logo (`Desktop/CAPRIO.svg`), replacing the wireframe-era placeholder nav built in Phase 1. `/` now renders `Header` with real content; everything below it stays a marked placeholder.

**What changed from the Phase 1 build**, now that the real design exists instead of a wireframe-based guess:

- **New `components/Logo.tsx`**: the wordmark inlined as an SVG component (not a `/public` file) so it can take `currentColor`, matching the pattern already used by `Button` secondary, `Chip`, and `Eyebrow` muted. `Header` gained an optional `logo` prop; `brand` still supplies the accessible name either way.
- **Nav content is now four plain links** (Activewear, Teamwear & Uniforms, Services, Our Factory), no mega menu — this resolves open question 6 in [04-product.md](04-product.md). `Header`'s mega-menu capability stays built and tested, just unused by real content; it's kept exercised via a dedicated fixture in `/styleguide`.
- **Nav link type corrected to Body** (18px, 400, not uppercase) — the real Figma link geometry measures as Body, not the bold caption style used in the earlier guess.
- **The desktop breakpoint dropped from a custom 1420px back to the standard `xl` (1280px)**, and now correctly covers all four required desktop viewports rather than two. The 1420px value (see the 1e entry below) was only ever needed because of how wide the placeholder six-link mega-menu nav was; the real four-link nav is roughly 200px narrower and fits inside a standard breakpoint. **This supersedes the 1e entry's breakpoint number below** — that entry is left as-is since it accurately describes what was true at the time.

**Verified:** real header measured at ~1117px intrinsic width; fits inside `xl` (1280px) with room to spare at all four required desktop viewports (1280, 1366, 1440, 1920), confirmed live, not assumed. Drawer confirmed at 768 and 1024. Mega menu Escape and drawer focus-trap/return re-tested via the styleguide fixture. `npx playwright test tests/screenshots.spec.ts` — 45 tests (styleguide + home, 15 viewports each), all passing, home now enabled in the suite. Build, types, lint clean. Visual comparison against the Figma screenshot at 1440 and 390 confirms the match.

**Not built yet:** hero and the other twelve sections.

### 2b · Nav spacing fixes, and headline type now scales past 1440 — 2026-08-22
Two quick owner-reported fixes on the nav, plus one design-system change that came out of the second one.

- **Logo-to-nav gap corrected to a real 40px.** It had been sitting inside a `justify-between` row with `nav` and `actions` as siblings, so the gap wasn't a fixed value at all — `justify-between` was distributing the row's leftover space into it, and it grew or shrank with the viewport. Fixed by grouping brand + nav into one wrapper with its own `gap-10`, so `justify-between` now only ever sees two things (that group, and `actions`) and leaves the 40px alone.
- **Nav link colour corrected to solid white.** `text-paper/80` (80% opacity) was standing in for the resting state; that's now full `text-paper`, with the dim moving to hover instead (`hover:opacity-80`).
- **Headline-scale type tokens now keep scaling above 1440px, up to a new max at 1920, then hold flat.** Reported as "font looks small on 1920" — correct, because the Figma-adoption pass (1e) deliberately held every size flat above 1440, which is right for reading text but reads as small for a headline against a physically bigger, still-1440-capped canvas. Extended to Display, Heading 1 to 4, Stat Number and Stat Label only; reading/UI styles (Heading 5, Overline, Body, Body Large, Button, Button Small) are unaffected and stay fixed everywhere. See the updated table and rule in [02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md).

**Verified:** live-measured gap is 40px and colour is `rgb(255,255,255)` after a hard reload (confirmed the first report was a stale cache, not a real bug). `tests/typography.spec.ts` extended with 8 new assertions locking in the 1920 max and the flat-above-1920 ceiling for all seven headline tokens — 23 tests, all passing. Full 30-test viewport suite still passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2c · Hero, desktop and mobile together, from the real Figma design — 2026-08-22
First section built under the "both breakpoints together" workflow the owner chose this session, rather than a desktop pass followed by a mobile pass. New `components/sections/Hero.tsx` — the first file in a new `components/sections/` folder, since a section is a page-specific composition, not a reusable atom like the rest of `/components`.

Reading the real Hero (desktop node `322:1522`, mobile node `323:1648`) surfaced one sitewide fix, agreed with the owner before building: **desktop container padding corrected from 48px to 80px**, matching Figma's real measured content margin (confirmed on both the nav and Hero frames) — this shifts the already-shipped, already-reviewed nav slightly wider on both edges.

Real, confirmed differences between desktop and mobile, not guesses: mobile has one full-width CTA (no secondary "Download Catalog" button at all); the offerings ticker is `Marquee` on desktop but a plain vertical list on mobile (genuinely different layouts, not one component reflowing); the ticker's label is bold and sentence-case, not the muted uppercase eyebrow treatment first assumed, and its separator is a sparkle glyph, not the "/" used elsewhere. `Marquee` gained `labelVariant` and `separator` props for this rather than replacing its old defaults, since the compliance ticker (section 10) hasn't had its own real design read yet. `MediaPlaceholder` gained `showLabel` (default true) so the Hero's play-button caption doesn't double up with the placeholder's own.

**Flagged, not fixed:** the mobile Figma frame shows a pill-outlined "Menu" nav trigger with a visible label, different from the icon-only `MobileNav` trigger already shipped and reviewed. Left alone since it's a nav change, out of scope for a Hero task, and the nav was already confirmed once.

**Verified:** screenshotted the built Hero at 390 and 1440 against the pulled Figma screenshots — matches closely; one cosmetic H1 line-wrap difference at exactly 832px/64px/400-weight remains (confirmed via computed styles that width, size and weight are all pixel-identical to Figma; the wrap difference is Figma's text layout engine measuring the glyphs slightly differently from a real browser, not a bug here — a forced line break would be more fragile than leaving it to reflow naturally). Full 30-viewport suite and 23-test typography suite both still passing. Confirmed the mobile list and desktop marquee never both render, switching cleanly at exactly `xl` (1280px). Build, types, lint clean.

### 2d · Hero review fixes — 2026-08-23
Six owner-reported fixes after reviewing 2c across viewports, all re-checked against Figma rather than adjusted by eye.

1. **Secondary "Download Catalog" CTA removed from the banner entirely**, both breakpoints — an earlier pass had it on desktop, per Figma's original frame; the owner's direct correction overrides that reading.
2. **`MediaPlaceholder` radius and border, fixed at the design-system level**, not just for Hero. The shell carried the real radius, but the absolutely-positioned fill/overlay/image inside it didn't inherit it, so a "rounded" box quietly rendered square-cornered content — fixed with `rounded-[inherit]` on those children (see the new note in [02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md)). The dark-tone border was `border-line-dark` (12% white), a structural-hairline strength that reads as invisible against a near-black placeholder on a near-black page; corrected to `border-paper/20`.
3. **Offerings ticker restructured to match Figma's real layout**: label stacked above the ticker row (was rendering beside it), item text corrected to regular-weight Body (was a bold caption style), separator icon moved to lead each item rather than trail it, and spacing corrected to the real measured values (40px between items, 12px between an item's icon and its text). `Marquee` gained a `layout` prop (`stacked` is now the default) alongside the `labelVariant`/`separator` props added in 2c.
4. **Mobile eyebrow corrected to ~16px**, down from the shared Overline token's fixed 20px — Figma's real mobile Hero measures it smaller. `Eyebrow` gained a `size` override prop for this rather than changing the shared token, since no other section's mobile eyebrow has been confirmed yet.
5. **Mobile ticker item text corrected to Heading 5 (24px)**, up from Body Large (20px) — Figma's real mobile list measures noticeably larger than what was first built. Spacing corrected to match: 32px from the title to the first item, 12px between items, 48px from the video above to the title.
6. **Mobile video confirmed genuinely edge-to-edge** — `hero.videoWrap` re-implements `.container-p`'s max-width and centring with responsive padding (none below `xl`) directly, since `.container-p` is a hand-written CSS class and can't be conditionally applied per breakpoint the way Tailwind utilities can.

**Verified:** every fix re-measured live via computed styles, not eyeballed — video box confirmed `x:0, width:390` (full viewport) at mobile; inner placeholder border confirmed `1px solid` white at 20% opacity with `16px` radius; mobile eyebrow confirmed `16px`; mobile ticker gaps confirmed `32px` (title to list) and `12px` (item to item) with item text at `24px`/`500`. Full 30-viewport suite and 23-test typography suite both still passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2f · Marquee top divider removed — 2026-08-23
The 2e divider sat with zero gap against the video's bottom edge, reading as the video box's own border rather than a marker for the ticker section below it. `marquee.base` changed from `border-y` to `border-b` — the bottom divider (separating the ticker from whatever comes next) stays. Also confirmed live: `Button`'s height is a flat `44px` (`min-h-11`, the sitewide tap-target floor) regardless of variant. Verified: marquee's top/bottom border widths `0px`/`1px`; full 30-viewport suite passing.

### 2g · Button height corrected to 54px; a real hide/show bug found and fixed — 2026-08-24
Three owner-reported items, one of which was a genuine bug rather than a design correction.

1. **`Button`'s height corrected to 54px** (was `min-h-11`, 44px), matching Figma's actual Hero banner buttons (node `322:1528` and children). The sitewide 44px floor stays as the accessibility minimum everywhere else; Button now exceeds it, matching the real design, and this is a design-system-level fix so every button site-wide picks it up automatically.
2. **Ticker bottom padding corrected to 32px** (was 16px).
3. **The mobile "Download Catalog" bug was real, not a caching issue** (unlike the 2b nav-gap report, which was). The button was hidden using `hidden xl:inline-flex` passed straight to `Button`, but `Button`'s own base class already includes an unconditional `inline-flex` — same specificity, same (absent) breakpoint prefix as `hidden`, so which one won came down to Tailwind's internal stylesheet ordering, not the class list. It happened to resolve correctly before, and flipped losing after an unrelated change (`min-h-11` → `min-h-[54px]`) shifted that ordering. **Fixed properly**: the show/hide toggle now lives on a wrapping element (`hidden xl:contents`), never on `Button` itself, so it can't race against Button's own base class. Checked the rest of the codebase for the same pattern (`className` passed to `Button` containing `hidden`) — this was the only instance.

**Verified:** live-measured — Hero button height `54px`; ticker bottom padding `32px`; "Download Catalog" (both the header's copy and Hero's) confirmed hidden at 390px width via `offsetParent`, visible at 1440px. Full 30-viewport suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

**Worth remembering:** passing `hidden <breakpoint>:<display-utility>` as a `className` to any shared component that sets its own unconditional display utility in its base recipe (`Button` does; check before adding more) is fragile — the fix is always to hide the wrapper, never fight the component's own base class.

### 2h · Root cause of the repeated type-size corrections, fixed — 2026-08-24
Four more owner-reported type/radius fixes, plus the actual process fix behind why they kept happening.

**Root cause:** text sizes were being estimated from Figma box-height ÷ an assumed 1.2 line-height ratio, then pattern-matched to the nearest of the 13 Figma type tokens — instead of treating a confirmed off-scale value as its own thing. This produced two wrong numbers in a row for the same ticker item text (first 20px, then 24px; the real value is 30px), and a video radius that was never actually checked against Figma at all (defaulted to `lg` when the section was first built).

Fixed, all four given directly by the owner rather than re-derived:

1. **Desktop ticker label** ("Fully Custom Offerings"): 22px/600 semibold. `marquee.labelBold` — a genuine one-off, not a token.
2. **Video corner radius**: 0 at both breakpoints. `MediaPlaceholder` gained a `radius="none"` option; Hero now passes it.
3. **Mobile ticker label**: 20px/500 medium (was riding the desktop 22/600 value). New one-off, `hero.tickerMobileLabel`.
4. **Mobile ticker items**: 30px, 400 regular. First attempt used `text-h3` (30/500 — but only at 1440px, since H3 is fluid; at mobile widths it computed to ~22px), corrected to a fixed one-off value; the weight was then also wrong (500 medium) until a follow-up correction to 400 regular (2026-08-24).

**Process change, documented in [02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md):** not every Figma text layer maps to the 13-style scale. A confirmed off-scale value is recorded as its own named recipe entry, never rounded to the nearest token and never estimated from box-height math. And before reusing any token's value at a specific width, check whether that token is fluid — a fluid token matching a number at one width is not the same as a fixed requirement.

**Verified:** live-measured all four — desktop label `22px`/`600`; mobile label `20px`/`500`; mobile items `30px`/`500` (confirmed only after the `text-h3` mistake was caught by this same verification step); video radius `0px` at both breakpoints. Full 30-viewport suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2i · Marquee loop-seam bug fixed — 2026-08-24
Owner-reported: the ticker restarted from "Design" after "Packaging" with no gap, unlike every other item transition. Two compounding bugs, not one:

1. **The 40px item gap was scoped to each pass's own `<ul>`, not to the seam between the two duplicated passes.** The track holds two `<ul>`s side by side with no gap of its own between them, so the boundary between pass one's last item and pass two's first had zero spacing. Fixed by giving every pass a `pr-10` trailing spacer, so the same 40px that separates every other item pair now separates the two passes too.
2. **The two passes weren't actually the same width**, which is a real bug beyond the visible gap: the leading icon was deliberately omitted from only the very first item overall (matching Figma's static screenshot), which meant pass one had 8 icons and pass two had 9 — an asymmetry that made the seamless-loop `-50%` transform land 20px into a gap rather than exactly on the boundary. Fixed by giving every item a leading icon uniformly, including each pass's first — a barely-noticeable difference from the static screenshot, but the only way the loop math can be exact.

**Verified:** live-measured — both passes render at identical widths; icon count matches (9/9) in each; the seam gap equals every other gap exactly (40px, not eyeballed). Full 30-viewport suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2j · Client logos (section 4) — 2026-08-24
Built from real Figma (desktop node `341:1732`, mobile node `343:1840`), read directly rather than composed from assumptions. First light (paper-background) section on the homepage — everything before it is on ink.

**Confirmed from Figma metadata, not eyeballed**: 8 real client names, exact 72px gaps between every logo pair (consistent across all 7 gaps measured), full colour (not greyscale) at both breakpoints, no eyebrow/label above the logos at either breakpoint (correcting the earlier wireframe-era "PRODUCING FOR BRANDS WORLDWIDE" guess in `content/home.ts`, which had no real design behind it). Desktop and mobile show the same 8 logos in a genuinely different order — a left-to-right ticker vs. a 2-column, row-major grid — so both real orders are kept in content rather than reusing one list.

**One number needed the owner directly**: the desktop Figma frame had been temporarily widened (to show logos hidden past 1440px), which made its overall height untrustworthy for section padding even though its width was still reliable for the logo positions and gaps. Given directly: 120px top / 0px bottom desktop, 40px both mobile.

**`Marquee` extended, not replaced**: `separator="none"` (this ticker has no glyph between logos, just space) and `gap="loose"` (72px, vs. the offerings ticker's 40px `default`) — both opt-in, so the offerings ticker's existing behaviour is untouched.

**New section `components/sections/ClientLogos.tsx`**: desktop is `Marquee`; mobile is a genuine 2-column CSS grid, not `LogoRow`, since `LogoRow`'s flowing wrap can't guarantee exactly 2 per row the way `grid-cols-2` can. Real brand names appear in Figma, but showing actual logo artwork needs per-brand permission not yet confirmed (open question 4) — logos render as the same bordered text placeholder every other unset logo already uses.

**Verified:** live-measured — desktop gap `72px` (both checked pairs); desktop padding `120px`/`0px`; mobile padding `40px`/`40px`; mobile item count `8` in the grid; the two duplicated marquee passes still render at identical widths (loop symmetry intact after adding `gap="loose"`); desktop and mobile layouts never both render. Full 30-viewport suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2k · Real client logos — 2026-08-24
Permission confirmed (open question 4, resolved). Real artwork now live, replacing the text placeholders from 2j.

**Sourced directly from Figma, not the open web**: `download_assets` against each logo's own individual node (`341:1734`–`341:1741`), one call per node rather than trusting the parent frame's aggregated raw-image list — that list mixed in unrelated raster fills (RISE's actual mark turned out to be a vector shape in Figma; the "raw image" the aggregate call associated with it was an unrelated near-blank texture) and would have risked shipping the wrong asset for at least one brand. Per-node export guarantees the correct, correctly-cropped mark every time. Caught and corrected one misread along the way: "OLFpak" (misread off a small early screenshot) is actually **WOLFpak**, icon-only at both breakpoints (no wordmark), confirmed once its node was exported directly.

**A real layout bug surfaced by the swap, not by inspection**: the mobile grid reused the desktop ticker's logo class (fixed height, unlimited width) — fine in the ticker, where items sit side by side with room to breathe, but wrong in a fixed 2-column grid, where MyProtein's wide aspect ratio overflowed into the next column. Fixed with a mobile-specific class that caps both height and width, so every logo contains within its own cell regardless of its own proportions — matching Figma's fixed-box-per-logo mobile layout, which was always the real design, just not enforced by the first pass.

**Verified:** all 8 images confirmed loaded (`complete: true`) with correct alt text and matching intrinsic dimensions; every mobile grid cell checked programmatically for image overflow past its own bounds — none. Full 30-viewport suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2l · Client logos, round 2 — 2026-08-24
Five more owner-reported corrections after reviewing 2k. Genuine misses, not disagreements — real Figma details the first real-asset pass didn't check closely enough.

1. **Each logo now renders at its exact Figma display size**, not scaled to a shared height. `brandLogos.registry` entries were carrying the *source file's* intrinsic dimensions (e.g. MyProtein's file was a 2560×324 high-res substitute); switched every entry to each node's own real export size, and swapped the MyProtein file itself to its precisely-cropped export so the aspect ratio it renders at is exact, not a close-but-not-identical stand-in.
2. **Desktop ticker's horizontal inset corrected to 80px**, matching every other section. A first pass wrapped the ticker in its own `container-p` *on top of* `Marquee`'s own internal one — an easy mistake to miss since the visual symptom (extra padding) looks like "a bit more whitespace," not an obvious bug, until measured.
3. **The bottom divider removed** — `Marquee` gained a `divider` prop (default true, preserving the offerings ticker's existing border) so the client logos ticker can opt out, rather than that border being unconditional.
4. **WOLFpak reconfirmed icon-only on desktop** (already correct), but its **mobile asset turned out to be genuinely different** — the actual Figma fill for that grid cell is icon-plus-wordmark, not the same icon-only mark scaled down. Two assets now, `mobileOverride` in the registry, not one reused. Along the way, a name mixup: an early low-res crop read as "OLFpak"; the real, full-size source confirms it's **WOLFpak**.
5. **Mobile grid gained the bordered box around each logo** that the first pass omitted entirely — `clientLogos.mobileItem` now carries a fixed 67px height, `border-line`, and `rounded-md`, with each logo centred both ways inside it via `object-contain` sized against the box.

**How the WOLFpak/OLFpak mixup actually got resolved**, since it's a preventable class of error: the earlier pass exported per-node via `download_assets`, but for WOLFpak that per-node crop was tiny (58×57) and rendered the "W" ambiguously at that size. Re-exporting the same brand's *other* real asset (the mobile cell's larger icon-plus-wordmark fill) at proper resolution settled it beyond doubt. Lesson: a crop small enough to blur a single letter isn't small enough to trust for a brand name — check a second, larger source before concluding.

**Mid-task incident:** the dev server had stopped responding entirely between test runs (not a code regression — `curl` returned no connection at all). Restarted it directly; also found `.claude/launch.json` had been left in attach-only mode (`url` with no launch command) from earlier in the session, which meant the preview tooling couldn't have restarted it on its own if asked to. Restored a real launch command so that failure mode doesn't recur.

**Verified:** live-measured — MyProtein renders at exactly `226×29` (matching its natural size, confirming no distortion); WOLFpak desktop `58×57` icon-only; ticker container padding `80px` both sides; divider `0px`; gap between items still `72px` (untouched by the other fixes); mobile cell height `67px`, border `1px rgba(0,0,0,0.1)` (`border-line`), radius `12px`, logo centred within 3px tolerance on both axes. Full 53-test suite (30 viewport + 23 typography) passing after the server restart. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2m · Mobile client logos, round 3 — 2026-08-24
Two quick corrections after reviewing 2l's mobile grid.

1. **Box corners squared off** (`rounded-md` removed).
2. **Attempted to fix the doubled border with `gap: -1px`.** This did not actually work — see 2n, which corrects it for real.

### 2n · Mobile grid border, fixed for real — 2026-08-24
The border-doubling from 2m was still present after that pass — `gap` cannot go negative at all (CSS disallows it; the browser clamps to 0 with no error), so `gap-[-1px]` had silently done nothing, and the mismeasurement above missed it.

Two more approaches considered and rejected before landing on the right one, worth recording so they aren't retried: `outline` doesn't solve it either, since outline still belongs to one element at a time — two adjacent cells' own outlines still end up side by side, the same problem as two borders. Negative margin (the classic table-border-collapse trick) works in principle but requires compensating the outer padding on every edge, which conflicts with `container-p`'s own responsive value (20/32/80 across breakpoints) — hardcoding a fixed compensation would have been correct at one breakpoint and wrong at the others.

**What actually works, cleanly**: each cell draws only its own right and bottom border (`mobileItem`); a separate element (`mobileGrid`, split out from `mobileWrap`, which keeps only the section's own vertical padding) draws the outer top and left edge that no cell owns. Every line then exists exactly once, by construction — not by any offset or overlap trick that has to be measured to confirm it worked.

**Verified, this time against the actual geometry, not just a gap that read as zero either way:** cells confirmed to carry border only on their right/bottom (`0px` on top/left); the grid wrapper confirmed to carry the top/left border; the seam between adjacent cells confirmed at exactly `1px` (one line, not two, not zero); the grid's own border confirmed sitting exactly `1px` from the first cell (tight, not pushed away by padding). Full 30-viewport suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 2p · Wide-screen container cap (1920px+), scoped to Hero — 2026-08-23
Owner reported that content read small on very large monitors (1920, and reported alongside a separate 430px mobile report — the two are unrelated problems, see below). Diagnosed as two different things, not one: the 1920 case is a container-width perception issue, not a font-size issue — `.container-p` caps at 1440px regardless of viewport, so a 1920px screen shows the same 1440px column with ~240px empty margin on each side, which reads as "everything is small" without any type actually shrinking.

Piloted on Hero alone first (a scoped `.hero-wide-pilot` class, `min-width: 1920px`, `max-width: 1680px`) so the effect could be judged before committing further — confirmed via screenshot the Hero band visibly using more of a 1920px screen while Nav/Client Logos/Trust Signals below it stayed at 1440 for contrast. Owner confirmed the direction and initially asked for it applied everywhere, built and future, so it was briefly rolled into the shared `--container-page` token itself. **Reverted the same day**, one review later: at sitewide scale it read badly on Client Logos and Trust Signals — a wider column just spread that grid/text content thinner rather than "using the space" the way Hero's video block and short heading could. Back to `.hero-wide-pilot` scoped to Hero's `bannerInner`/`videoWrap` only; `--container-page` itself stays a flat 1440px everywhere.

**Standing rule from this**: widening the container past 1440 is a per-section visual judgement call, confirmed by looking at it, not something every section should inherit automatically from using `.container-p`. A future section only gets the wide treatment if it's actually checked and reads well wide — large media/visual bands are the likely candidates, not grid or text-heavy sections.

1680px chosen as the flat cap where it does apply, not a further fluid scale past 1920: real CSS viewports past 2560 are rare in practice (most 4K/5K displays run 150-200% OS scaling, reporting an effective CSS width closer to 1920-2560 than their raw pixel count), and 2560 is a real minority of traffic unlike anything past it, so 1680 leaves it some room without over-engineering a scale for viewports that barely occur.

**Verified**: `--container-page` computed value confirmed back to `1440px` at 1920px viewport; Hero's two `.hero-wide-pilot` elements confirmed at `1680px`; every other `.container-p` element on the homepage confirmed at `1440px` (the handful reading `0px` are the mobile-only wraps correctly hidden at this width). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing, no overflow, re-run after the revert. Build, types, lint clean.

The 430px mobile report is a separate, not-yet-addressed problem (fluid type curve's low-end anchor, not a container issue) — next up.

### 2u · Certified & Compliant, owner corrections round 1 — 2026-08-23
Three more issues found the same day, one from an owner request and two from re-verifying against it.

- **Mobile logos were the wrong height** whenever a logo's mobile crop has a different aspect ratio than its own source file — confirmed on BSCI, which rendered 119×102 (scaled by the source file's own 156:134 ratio) instead of its real 119×43. Root cause: Tailwind's preflight sets `img { height: auto }`, which silently overrides `next/image`'s intended height attribute. Desktop never hit this, since every desktop size already matches its source file's intrinsic ratio exactly. Fixed with an explicit inline `style` width/height on the mobile `<Image>` — inline style always wins over any stylesheet rule, unlike a class, so this is the reliable fix rather than a specificity guess.
- **The mobile grid sat 43px in from the section edges, not the intended 20px**: `certified.mobileGridWrap` had no `w-full`, so under `root`'s `items-center` (a flex-column child doesn't stretch to fill by default) it shrank to its own content width and centred, leaving extra unintended margin beyond `container-p`'s real 20px inset. Fixed by adding `w-full`.

**Verified**: live-measured — desktop H2 height 128px (exactly 2 lines at the 64px line-height); mobile grid measured 20px from both the left and right section edges; all 6 mobile logos' rendered width/height matching their registry entries exactly (BSCI 119×43, not 119×102). ### 2x · All five section components prop-ified — 2026-08-24

Deliberately **not** building a fully generic, config-driven layout system on top of this (e.g. one component that renders any "media + text grid" pattern from a schema) — that would mean designing an API for a pattern only used once so far, before knowing whether a second real page wants the same layout, a subset of it, or a variant. The scalable path here is to prop-ify now (cheap, reversible) and only promote a section to a fully generic component once a second real page actually needs its exact layout — informed by two real examples instead of a guess, the same reasoning already applied to `Card`'s spec (see 2q's note on correcting Card's guessed values only once a real section used it).

### 3b · Mobile grid corner radius fixed for real, applied to Client Logos too — 2026-08-24
Owner sent a screenshot of ISO 9001's mobile tile: the 4px corner looked jagged and stair-stepped, not a smooth curve. Root cause: Certified & Compliant's mobile grid (and Client Logos' own, once it got a radius) split the outer border across two elements via edge-ownership (wrapper owns top+left, cells own right+bottom), then relied on a `rounded-[4px] overflow-hidden` wrapper to round the corners by clipping. That doesn't work — clipping a square-cornered border with a curved mask just cuts the straight line off at an angle; a border only renders a native, smooth curve when it and its `border-radius` live on the *same* element.

Owner also asked to apply the same 4px radius to Client Logos, which had been square this whole time.

Fixed both at once, since they share the identical recipe pattern: the entire outer rectangle is now **one border on `mobileGrid` itself** (all 4 sides), so its own `rounded-[4px]` renders natively and smoothly. Cells (`mobileItem`) no longer draw any outer edge at all — only the internal dividers between them, added conditionally per cell position (`mobileItemDividerRight` on left-column cells, `mobileItemDividerBottom` on every row except the last, computed in each section component from `index % 2` / `index >= length - 2`). This removes the clipping trick entirely rather than patching it.

**Verified**: live-measured Certified & Compliant's mobile grid — computed `border-radius: 4px` and `border-width: 1px` on `mobileGrid` itself (one native border, not a clip); per-cell border widths confirmed matching the intended pattern exactly (left-column cells 1px right border except none on the rightmost column, every row but the last 1px bottom border, last row none). ### 3e · Stats, desktop background bleed and layout width fixes — 2026-08-24
Two real bugs, both from the initial desktop implementation.

**Background stopped at 1440px, not edge to edge**: `bg-ink` had been on the same element as `container-p`, which caps at `max-width: 1440px` — so on any wider viewport, the black fill itself stopped at 1440px, showing the page's default background outside it instead of true edge-to-edge black. Owner explicitly flagged this shouldn't be constrained to a fixed width. Fixed by splitting the background onto a separate, unconstrained outer wrapper (`stats.desktopOuter`) with `container-p` moved to an inner content wrapper — the same split Hero already used (background on the outer `<section>`, `container-p` on an inner wrapper), which Stats just hadn't followed. Confirmed live at 1920px: the black fill now spans the full viewport width, not 1440px with white margins outside it.

**The caption text column was the wrong width**: `stats.desktopList` had been `flex-1`, stretching to fill whatever space was left after the 660px media block — at a 1280px content area that's roughly 490px, not Figma's real, fixed 300px column, so captions ran noticeably wider and wrapped to fewer lines than the real design. Re-checking the geometry also surfaced a second error in the same area: the media-to-text gap had been implemented as 84px, actually Trust Signals' own gap value, mistakenly reused here instead of this section's real, different gap. Correct math (`get_metadata` on node `387:501`): image right edge 835, text starts at 965, gap **130px** — and the whole 1090px group turns out to be centred within the standard 1280px content area (95px margin beyond `container-p`'s own 80px padding, symmetric both sides), not flush against the left edge with the text stretching into whatever's left. Fixed: `desktopList` is now a fixed `w-[300px]`, the gap corrected to `130px`, and `desktopInner` centred via `mx-auto w-fit` instead of stretching full-width.

**Verified**: live-measured at 1920px — `desktopOuter` width `1920px` (full viewport, was capped at 1440); at 1440px, `1440px` (unaffected, matches viewport either way). Media+text group width `1090px`, text column `300px` exactly, gap `130px` exactly, caption text confirmed unchanged/correct. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing, no overflow. Build, types, lint clean.

### 3f · Inside the Factory (section 9) — 2026-08-24
Read the real Figma frames (desktop `402:904`, mobile `402:914`). The third dark section (Hero, Stats, now this). `content/home.ts`'s existing `insideFactory.body`/`.cta` were dropped: the real frame is a centred heading plus an image gallery only, nothing else — the same wireframe-era-guess pattern that hit Trust Signals' first pass.

The owner shared an animation reference (a Shopify marketing page video, `reference/WhatsApp Video 2026-08-24 at 20.03.32.mp4`) for the mobile gallery's interaction. Getting to it took a few real detours worth recording: the file lived in `~/Downloads`, which every tool available (Bash, Read, the browser's `file://` navigation) was blocked from reading by macOS's own per-process sandbox permission — not something fixable by the user simply saying "ok" in chat, since it requires an actual OS permission grant tied to the specific process. The reliable fix was the owner manually copying the file into the project's own `reference/` folder via Finder (which runs under their own permissions), after which it became readable. No `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` were available either, so frames were extracted with a small ad-hoc Swift script using `AVFoundation` (`AVAssetImageGenerator`) instead — a reasonable fallback given the tools actually present on the machine.

Reading the frames first suggested an auto-rotating carousel (content changed between samples with no visible drag). Asked the owner directly rather than assuming; they corrected it: they were swiping it by hand the whole time, the finger just wasn't visible in the recording. So this is a manual, finger-swipeable carousel only — no timer, no auto-advance.

Built as native CSS scroll-snap (`overflow-x-auto` + `snap-x snap-mandatory` + `snap-center` per card), not custom JS drag-tracking — smooth finger-drag-following and automatic snap-to-centre for free, correct at every mobile viewport width, not just Figma's exact 360px reference. Confirmed via `get_metadata` that cards touch with zero gap and the "peek" is pure viewport clipping of the full-size neighbouring card, not a separately-cropped element — the track's own `padding-inline: calc((100% - 300px) / 2)` lets the first/last cards centre too. Added a new `.no-scrollbar` utility to `app/globals.css` (no cross-engine Tailwind equivalent exists) to hide the native scrollbar while keeping touch scrolling live.

Both breakpoints' headings use a genuinely custom eyebrow colour matching neither of `Eyebrow`'s built-in tones — built as bespoke markup rather than `SectionHeading`, to avoid stacking a custom colour utility against `SectionHeading`'s own `tone` class (the same cascade-order risk already found and fixed twice this session).

**A real overflow bug, caught by the Playwright suite, not by inline verification**: the desktop gallery's 3 fixed-width tiles (195+950+195+2×48 gaps = 1436px) overflowed at 1280px and 1366px viewports, since `shrink-0` prevented them from ever narrowing. Re-checking the geometry while fixing it also surfaced that the gallery had incorrectly been nested inside the heading's `container-p`-padded wrapper — Figma's real gallery is full-bleed, spanning the frame's entire width, unlike the heading above it. Fixed both: removed `shrink-0` (flexbox's default `flex-shrink: 1` now lets all 3 tiles shrink together, proportionally, so the row always fits), and moved the gallery to its own full-bleed sibling element instead of a padded child.

**Verified**: live-measured — desktop tile widths exactly 195/950/195 at 1440px (proportionally smaller with no overflow at 1366px, confirmed `scrollWidth === clientWidth`); gallery wrapper spans the full viewport width at both sizes; heading colours match Figma exactly. Mobile: section padding 48px/48px internal, 72px external margin; carousel track padding-inline 37.5px at 375px viewport (matching `(375-300)/2` exactly); card width 300px, zero gap between cards; a simulated scroll to the second card landed and centred correctly. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (after the overflow fix), no overflow. Build, types, lint clean.

### 3g · Sitewide eyebrow-colour rule; Inside the Factory owner corrections — 2026-08-24
Four corrections in one round, the biggest being a genuine sitewide standing rule.

**Desktop heading now wraps to 2 lines**, the same fix already applied to Certified & Compliant: the real heading column is 812px wide (node `402:906`, the identical value), passed via `headingClassName` — unconstrained it ran to 1 line.

**A new, permanent sitewide rule: an eyebrow paired with a heading is always `#ABB5C0` on a dark section, always `#17191E` on a light one — no exceptions, including Hero.** Owner explicitly confirmed the scope after being asked directly, since it meant overriding Hero's eyebrow (previously accent orange, confirmed against real Figma across several earlier rounds) — a case worth confirming rather than guessing, given undoing already-verified work is costly if wrong. `Eyebrow`'s `tone` prop lost its `accent`/`muted` shape and its default entirely: it's now `"dark" | "light" | "muted"`, required, no fallback, so a caller can't silently inherit the wrong colour. `SectionHeading` gained a matching required `eyebrowTone` prop. Updated every real call site: Hero (`dark`), What We Make (`light`), Certified & Compliant (`light`), Inside the Factory (`dark`), plus the two styleguide demo blocks. This also **simplified** Inside the Factory's heading: the bespoke markup built earlier specifically to avoid a colour-class conflict was no longer needed, since `dark` tone is now exactly the colour this section needs — reverted to `SectionHeading` directly.

**Mobile eyebrow is now centred**, via `SectionHeading`'s existing `align="center"` (this section's mobile heading previously centred its text but not the eyebrow block itself, a real gap between the intended and actual result).

**The mobile carousel's active card now renders taller than its neighbours — a real overlap effect, not the flat filmstrip built in 3f.** Re-reading the Figma geometry more closely after the owner flagged the mobile result as "way different than the design" showed the active card is 300×340 while the flanking cards are a shorter 300×248, both vertically centred — not a uniform card size with the ends merely clipped by the viewport, which is what 3f had assumed. Since every card becomes "active" as the user swipes through in turn, this can't be pre-baked into static CSS. `InsideFactory.tsx` became the project's **first client component** (`"use client"`): a `scroll` listener on the track computes the nearest-centred card (`Math.round(scrollLeft / cardWidth)`, exact since cards have zero gap), throttled with `requestAnimationFrame`, and swaps that card's `MediaPlaceholder` `ratio` between two new exact values (`15:17` active, `75:62` inactive) rather than setting an explicit height — the resize is driven by `aspect-ratio`, so there's no separate height state to keep in sync with which card is active.

**Verified**: live-measured — desktop heading height 128px (2 lines); Inside the Factory, Hero eyebrows both `rgb(171, 181, 192)` (`#ABB5C0`); Certified & Compliant, What We Make eyebrows both `rgb(23, 25, 30)` (`#17191E`). Mobile: eyebrow `text-align: center` and its parent `align-items: center`; card heights confirmed `[340, 248, 248, 248, 248]` at rest (card 0 active) and correctly shifted to `[248, 248, 340, 248, 248]` after a simulated scroll to card index 2 — the active card follows the scroll position exactly. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing, no overflow. Build, types, lint clean.

### 3h · Inside the Factory, smoother carousel motion — 2026-08-24
Owner flagged the mobile carousel as jerky. Root cause: 3g's active/inactive height swap was a two-state jump at a rounded scroll index (`Math.round(scrollLeft / cardWidth)`), so a card popped between 340px and 248px at a threshold instead of resizing continuously with the drag. Replaced with continuous, per-frame interpolation: each card's height is now `lerp(340, 248, distanceFromCentre)`, computed every scroll frame for every card and written straight to each card's DOM `style.height` via refs (not React state), so a 60fps drag never waits on a re-render. `MediaPlaceholder` gained an optional `style` prop for this (an explicit height always wins over its `aspect-ratio` class with no conflict, since a definite width + height leaves nothing for `aspect-ratio` to resolve).

**Verified**: at rest, heights confirmed `[340, 248, 248, 248, 248]`; after a snap-scroll to index 2, `[248, 248, 340, 248, 248]` — continuous logic still lands correctly at rest positions. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing, no overflow. Build, types, lint clean.

### 3i · Inside the Factory, fixed the "background moving" regression from 3h — 2026-08-24
Owner: "the entire black background is also moving when I am sliding the image." Root cause: `mobileTrack`'s own height was intrinsic (sized to its tallest child), so 3h's per-frame `card.style.height` writes on 5 cards every scroll frame also changed the track's own box every frame, which reflowed the whole section (and everything below it) — reading as the entire dark background shifting during the swipe, not just the intended cards resizing. Fix: pinned `mobileTrack` to a fixed `h-[340px]` (the active card's own height), so cards now grow/shrink *inside* a box that itself never changes size, with zero effect on surrounding layout.

**Verified**: live DOM check — track's `getBoundingClientRect()` and the section's `getBoundingClientRect()` are byte-identical before and after a scroll-position change (both height `340px`/`677px` unchanged). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 3r · Two bugs reported on a real Samsung Galaxy Z Fold7 — 2026-08-25
Owner testing on-device found two problems: (1) `ScrollSpotlightList` (Hero's offerings list, Final CTA's compliance list) showed no animation at all; (2) Inside the Factory's mobile carousel had visibly wrong layout when animating — and clarified this second one isn't Fold-specific, it reproduces at other large mobile viewports too.

(1) Not reproducible in this project's own Chromium-based browser testing (the spotlight worked correctly at every width tried, including 800px). The likely real cause: some mobile browsers (Samsung Internet included) batch/throttle native `scroll` events far more coarsely during momentum/fling scrolling than desktop Chrome does, which no amount of testing from a desktop browser can surface. Fixed defensively rather than by guessing at Samsung Internet's exact behaviour: replaced the `scroll`-event listener with a persistent, self-scheduling `requestAnimationFrame` loop that recalculates every animation frame unconditionally — this removes any dependency on whether or how often `scroll` fires, at the cost of a few extra `getBoundingClientRect` reads per frame (negligible for a handful of list items).

(2) This one *was* reproducible directly: `InsideFactory`'s mobile carousel is `xl:hidden`, so it also renders at tablet-range widths (700-1024px), where its centring formula `px-[calc((100%-300px)/2)]` produces a huge side inset (250px at 800px wide) — wide enough that a second full card becomes visible next to the first, turning the intended "one active card, peeking neighbours" carousel into what reads as a broken 2-up grid. Fixed by capping the padding at the design's own confirmed ~40px peek: `px-[min(40px,calc((100%-300px)/2))]`. Below ~380px wide the formula's own value is already under 40px, so narrow-phone centring is unaffected.

**Verified**: live-measured at 800px width — track padding dropped from 250px to 40px, screenshot confirms a proper one-active/two-peeking card layout instead of a 2-up grid; re-checked at 375px width and confirmed unchanged (37.5px padding, no regression). `ScrollSpotlightList`'s rAF-loop fix can't be verified against the actual reported bug (real Samsung Internet, not reproducible here) — the owner will need to re-test on the physical device. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 3z · Client Logos ticker polish, Hero label weight — 2026-08-26

Fixed with a deliberately chosen, tight `max-width` (200px) on an `inline-block` nested inside the actual flex-item `<h2>` wrapper — a plain `<span>` was tried first and ignored `max-width` entirely, since a bare inline element doesn't respect width/max-width at all. ### 3aa · How It Works chevron, tracks Y too, no more jerky entrance — 2026-08-26
Two related owner reports on the desktop chevron from 3t. (1) "Not really smooth... feels jerky" on entering the section: the actual bug was a stale-position jump, not a missing/wrong animation — `mouseenter` only set opacity before, leaving the chevron's transform at whatever it was last (or its `0,0` default on first load), so it faded in in the wrong spot and then visibly snapped to the real cursor on the next `mousemove`. Fixed by seeding the transform from the `mouseenter` event's own coordinates before opacity turns on, so there's nothing to snap from. (2) "Chevron should be available even when I hover over the text, currently it is only for the media containers" — the chevron only ever tracked the cursor's X position, pinned to a fixed Y (`top-[110px]`, centred on the 220px media block only), so hovering the card's title/body below the image never brought it anywhere near the cursor. Now tracks Y continuously too, exactly like X already did — `top-0 left-0` is just the transform's origin, not a resting position, same idea as the click-zone iterations from 3t.

**Verified**: live-measured — dispatched a mousemove ~30px above the wrap's bottom edge (over the text zone of a card, well below the image) and confirmed the chevron's `transform` Y-offset matches the cursor's real position there (323px measured vs 324px expected), not the old fixed 110px. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 3ab · How It Works chevron, clamped to stop edge-clipping — 2026-08-26
Owner report, immediately after 3aa's Y-tracking fix: entering the section "gives an impression that it's coming from the top," and moving down "the chevron gets hidden in the container." Root cause: `desktopScrollerWrap` clips overflow, and now that the chevron's Y can reach the full card height (not just a safe mid-height band like before 3aa), the 80px circle got hard-clipped by that overflow the instant its centre came within 40px of any edge — top, bottom, left, or right. That clipping was what read as a "coming from"/"hiding in" effect, not a real show/hide transition.

Fixed by clamping both `x` and `y` in `positionChevron` (`Math.min(Math.max(coord, CHEVRON_HALF), dimension - CHEVRON_HALF)`) so the full circle always stays inside the box, at every cursor position including right at an edge. `opacity` (200ms, already in place) is now the only thing that ever visibly changes on enter/leave — there's nothing left for the overflow clip to catch.

**Verified**: live-measured — dispatched cursor positions at all four edges (1px from top/bottom/left/right) and confirmed the chevron's `transform` keeps the circle fully inside the wrap every time (e.g. top edge → `translateY: 0px`, spanning 0-80px of a 394px-tall wrap; bottom edge → `translateY: 314px`, spanning 314-394px, never crossing the boundary; same confirmed left/right against the 1440px-wide wrap). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

Root cause: `.marquee-edge-fade`'s `mask-image` used percentage stops (`black 8%, black 92%`), which scale with the track's own width — correct-looking at the width it was tuned on, but widening on anything bigger. Fixed the same way as the top (`-mt-10`, from 4b) — added `-mb-8` alongside it on the same `<Marquee>` instance, cancelling both of `marquee.base`'s own paddings so the wrapper's own `pt-[40px]`/`gap-8`/`pb-[40px]` are the only things producing visible space.

### 3ac · How It Works chevron, transition-all instead of transition-opacity — 2026-08-26
Owner report, immediately after 3ab's edge-clamping fix: still jerky on both entrance and exit. Root cause: `transform` had zero CSS transition at all — every single `mousemove` sample (a browser event, not a fixed animation frame rate) teleported the circle instantly to that exact pixel with no easing between samples. That's inherently jerky/discontinuous motion for a cursor-following element regardless of the opacity fade being correct, and it's most visible at the very first frame (entry, right as `mouseenter` seeds the starting position) and the very last one (exit, wherever the circle happened to be when `mouseleave` fired).

Fixed by widening the transition from `transition-opacity duration-200 ease-out` to a single `transition-all duration-150 ease-out` covering both `opacity` and `transform` together, turning per-pixel teleporting into a smooth trailing glide. Click accuracy is unaffected — the click handler reads `directionRef`, set from the real cursor position on every `mousemove`, not from this element's own (now deliberately lagging) visual position.

**Verified**: live-measured — `getComputedStyle` on the chevron confirms `transition-property: all`, `transition-duration: 0.15s`, `transition-timing-function: cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.2, 1)` (ease-out), replacing the old opacity-only transition. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. Build, types, lint clean.

### 4d · Client Logos mobile marquee: title-clipping bug fixed for real, plus Hero's own extra 72px gap removed — 2026-08-26
Owner reported two things after 4c: the title's own bottom (its descenders) looked cut off, and there was more than 40px of space between the black Hero box above and this section.

**The clipping was a real, visible bug caused by 4c's own fix, not a font/line-height issue.** 4c had cancelled `Marquee`'s internal `pt-10`/`pb-8` with a negative margin (`className="-mt-10 -mb-8"`) to reclaim the 40/32px of built-in padding. That shifted the `Marquee` box itself (including its own opaque `toneLight`/`bg-paper` background) up by 40px without changing where the flex layout had placed it relative to the gap-8 above it — net effect, the box's own top edge ended up 8px higher than the reserved gap, and since later DOM siblings paint over earlier ones at the same stacking level, that box's own background painted directly over the title's descenders. Reclaiming space by moving a box with a negative margin, when that box carries its own opaque background, isn't equivalent to actually removing the padding.

**Real fix**: gave `Marquee` a proper `padded` prop (default `true`, using the padding already in `marquee.basePaddingDefault`; `false` uses the new `marquee.basePaddingNone`, `pt-0 pb-0`) instead of a class string doing double duty as both a size override and a position hack. `ClientLogos.tsx`'s `MobileMarquee` now passes `padded={false}` — the box's own edges never move relative to its flex position, so there's nothing left to paint over the title. `marquee.base` itself no longer carries any padding directly; it lives entirely in the two new mutually exclusive `basePaddingDefault`/`basePaddingNone` keys, so every other `Marquee` consumer (defaulting to `padded=true`) is unaffected.

**The extra gap was Hero's own mobile section margin, unrelated to Client Logos' own recipe.** `hero.section` carried `max-xl:mb-[72px]` (the standard mobile section-to-section gap, contributed via margin since Hero has its own full-bleed background and can't use padding for this). That stacked with Client Logos' own explicit `pt-[40px]`, for 112px total above the title, not the requested 40px — present since Client Logos' mobile wrapper first got its own 40px top padding (2026-08-26, this round's own docs entry above), just not previously flagged. Since Client Logos is the only section that ever follows Hero, `hero.section`'s mobile margin is now `mb-0` — Client Logos' own top padding is the sole source of that gap. A future section following Hero that wants the *standard* 72px gap would supply it itself via the usual `pt-0`/`pb-[72px]` pattern, same as every other section-to-section gap.

**Verified**: live-measured on the real homepage at mobile width — wrapper top padding `40`px, title-to-marquee gap `32`px, wrapper bottom padding `40`px, Hero-to-wrapper gap `0`px (so total space above the title is exactly the intended `40`px), title renders with no clipping (screenshot-confirmed). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. ### 4h · Chevron mechanism extracted into a shared hook, replaced CSS-transition smoothing with a requestAnimationFrame easing loop — 2026-08-26
Owner report, after How It Works/Inside the Factory/Exhibitions had each shipped their own copy of the floating cursor-tracking chevron: still not "really smooth," and asked for it improved everywhere it's used, not just wherever was reported. By this point the same logic (positioning, clamping, opacity, click-to-page) and the same recipe classes existed verbatim in three files (`HowItWorks.tsx`, `InsideFactory.tsx`, `Exhibitions.tsx`) — a real duplication problem in its own right (per CLAUDE.md rule 5, "build once, reuse everywhere"), and exactly why the same smoothness ceiling had already been hit and "fixed" three separate times without ever actually being solved.

**Root cause of the remaining ceiling**: every prior fix (X+Y tracking, edge clamping, seeding position on `mouseenter`, `transition-all` instead of `transition-opacity`) was a real, correct fix for its own specific bug, but the underlying mechanism — write the raw cursor position to `transform` on every `mousemove`, let a CSS transition ease between samples — has a ceiling no amount of duration/easing tuning can cross. `mousemove` fires at an uneven, browser-decided rate (bursty under fast movement, sparse under slow), and a CSS transition re-eases from scratch every time its target changes mid-flight, which reads as a subtle stutter/rubber-band on quick or reversing motion rather than one continuous glide.

**The fix**: extracted the entire mechanism into `components/DesktopChevronScroller.tsx` — a `useDesktopChevronScroller(cardPitch)` hook plus a `DesktopChevron` presentational component, both now imported by all three sections instead of copy-pasted. The hook replaces the CSS-eased transform with a `requestAnimationFrame` loop that runs continuously while hovered: every frame, the chevron's rendered position eases a fixed fraction (`SMOOTHING = 0.35`) of the remaining distance toward the cursor's last known position (exponential smoothing), decoupling the visual motion entirely from `mousemove`'s own event rate — there is always a next frame to converge a little further, regardless of how sparse the underlying events are. The smoothing factor is scaled by each frame's real elapsed time (`1 - (1 - SMOOTHING) ** (dt / (1000/60))`), so motion converges at the same real-world speed on a 144Hz display as a 60Hz one, rather than trailing faster the higher the refresh rate. All the previously-fixed bugs carry over unchanged (X+Y clamping to `CHEVRON_HALF`, seeding `current = target` on `mouseenter` before the loop starts so there's no glide-in from a stale position, `pointer-events-none` on the circle). The chevron's own CSS now transitions `opacity` only (200ms) — a CSS transition on `transform` would just fight the per-frame JS writes, never settling, which is why it's removed entirely rather than layered on top of the rAF loop.

New shared `chevronScroller` recipe (`circle`/`icon`) in `components/ui/styles.ts` replaces the three sections' own identical `desktopChevronCircle`/`desktopChevronIcon` keys. Each section keeps only its own `cardPitch` (335+24, 950+48, 469+24) and its own track/card layout classes (`snap-start` vs `snap-center`, different padding formulas) — the genuine per-section differences, not the shared mechanism.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30) — no visual regression, since the chevron's resting/hidden state (opacity 0) and the row layouts are unchanged. `npm run build` clean. Live interactive verification (hover/mousemove path) hit the same synthetic-event-dispatch limitation already documented for this exact chevron on How It Works (2026-08-25) — this browser tool's dispatched `mousemove`/`mouseenter` events don't reach React's handlers here, a known environment limitation, not something this change could newly break; the underlying math and control flow were traced by hand instead (seed-on-enter, per-frame convergence, cleanup on unmount/leave).

### 4j · Header hides on scroll down, reveals on scroll up — 2026-08-26
Owner request: hide the sticky header past a certain scroll point, reveal it again on scrolling back up, threshold left to "best practice" judgement. Implemented directly in `Header.tsx` (single-use, not extracted into a shared hook the way the chevron mechanism was — nothing else on the site needs this pattern yet): a `scroll` listener, rAF-throttled, compares each sample's `window.scrollY` against the previous one for direction, and toggles a `navHidden` state that adds `header.hidden` (`-translate-y-full`, `transition-transform duration-300 ease-in-out` on `header.base`).

**Threshold**: the header's own *measured* height (`headerRef.current.offsetHeight`), not a hardcoded pixel guess — it never hides before the page has actually scrolled past where the header would sit anyway, correctly at whatever height the header renders at for the current breakpoint, rather than a single guessed constant that could be wrong at some viewport. This is the standard pattern used by most editorial/marketing sites for reclaiming vertical space on long pages without removing the nav outright (it's one upward scroll away).

**Two deliberate carve-outs, not part of the literal ask but necessary for it not to break existing interactions**: the header never hides while a mega menu or the mobile drawer is open (both force `navHidden` back to `false` on every scroll sample) — hiding the header out from under an open menu the user is actively interacting with would be a real usability regression, not a subtlety worth trading for the scroll effect. The header keeps its `sticky top-0` positioning the whole time; only its rendered offset (`translate`) moves, so the transition has something to animate and the header's own space in the document flow never disappears.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. This project's own browser tool hits the same synthetic-mouse-event limitation already documented for the chevron work, but scroll is a plain native DOM event (not part of React's synthetic pointer-event system), so a real Playwright session could drive and measure it directly: header's `boundingBox()` starts at `{y:0, height:87}`, moves to `{y:-87}` after scrolling down 1200px in three wheel steps, returns to `{y:0}` after scrolling up 150px, and stays at `{y:0}` for a small 40px scroll well under the 87px threshold — exactly the intended behaviour at each stage. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30). `npm run build` clean.

### 4k · Fixed Footer's sticky-reveal transition: it was covering the whole page, not just revealing near the end — 2026-08-26
Owner report right after 4i shipped: "footer seems like sticky on the bottom," confirmed on follow-up as "footer covers content." 4i's `sticky bottom-0` alone (matched against a quick read of `afternow.co/services/`) turned out to be an incomplete implementation of the reference site's effect, not a broken one — it was missing a second, necessary declaration.

**Root cause, isolated with a minimal zero-dependency test page** (a tall spacer div + a bare `position: sticky; bottom: 0` footer, no framework, no other CSS): a `bottom: 0` sticky element pins to the viewport's bottom edge for the *entire* scrollable range of the page, from the very first pixel — not only near the document's end, the assumption 4i shipped under. Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` at every scroll position from `0` to the page's max scroll: the box's screen position never changed. Separately, because a `position: sticky` element is always "positioned," and `footer.root` had no explicit `z-index`, it defaulted into the same stacking tier as any other `z-index: auto` positioned box — which paints *above* ordinary in-flow, non-positioned content (`<main>` and its sections) regardless of DOM order. Both effects together mean the footer was genuinely glued to the bottom of the viewport at all times, and un-beaten in the stacking order, so it covered whatever was supposed to be scrolling by underneath it — exactly the owner's report.

**The actual mechanism (verified in the same isolated test page before touching real code)**: the "reveal" comes from **stacking order**, not from sticky's timing. Give the scrolling content a higher stacking context than the sticky footer (`<main className="relative z-10">` in `app/page.tsx`) and the footer an explicit lower one (`footer.root` gained `z-0`). The footer is still technically pinned to the viewport bottom the whole time — but now `<main>`'s own opaque section backgrounds paint over it during ordinary scrolling, and it only becomes visible once main's real content has actually scrolled past that screen position (confirmed via `elementFromPoint` at the viewport's bottom edge: real page content at scroll `0`/`500`/`5000`/`12000`, the footer's own markup only at the true end, `~13330`). This is the same *visual* effect as `afternow.co`, produced by a different, correct mechanism — the original single-declaration read of that site was too literal.

**Debugging note**: this project's own browser preview tool proved unreliable mid-investigation (blank screenshots, a tab viewport silently collapsing to `0x0` after `navigate`, wildly inconsistent `document.body.scrollHeight` readings across otherwise-identical calls, all independent of this bug) — ruled out as the actual cause by testing against a freshly-rebuilt production server (`next build` + `next start` on a scratch port) in a brand-new tab, and separately confirming the exact same numeric behaviour on a completely isolated static HTML file outside this project entirely. Chasing that tooling noise cost real effort before landing on the true, reproducible root cause; noted here so a future "the preview tool disagrees with itself" moment isn't re-litigated from scratch.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `elementFromPoint` sweep at the viewport's bottom edge across 5 scroll positions confirms real content (not the footer) renders there until the true end of the page; a second sweep at the viewport's top edge confirms Header stays correctly visible throughout, including at max scroll. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30) on a clean rebuild. `npm run build` clean.

### 4l · 4k's fix was incomplete: the footer was still ghosting through light sections — 2026-08-26
Owner sent a screen recording (`reference/Screen Recording 2026-08-26 at 11.44.37 PM.mov`) taken minutes after 4k shipped, showing the bug still live: scrolling past Trust Signals and Certified & Compliant (both light sections) showed faint but readable footer nav-link and address text ghosted on top of them. Extracted representative frames from the recording (`opencv-python`, since no `ffmpeg` was available in this environment — installed once via `pip3`, not a project dependency) to confirm the ghosting was real and not a hunch.

**4k's fix was necessary but not sufficient.** `footer.root`'s `z-0` and `<main>`'s `relative z-10` correctly win the *stacking order* — confirmed again live, `elementsFromPoint` puts a Trust Signals element on top of Footer's own elements at the same screen coordinate. But winning the stacking order only re-orders which layer paints on top; it doesn't make a transparent layer opaque. Checked the actual computed `background-color` up the ancestor chain at a ghosted point: `<main>` and Trust Signals' own wrapper `div`/`section` are all `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` — genuinely transparent. Only `<body>` (`bg-paper`) supplies a real background, and body sits *underneath* both `<main>` (z-10) and Footer (z-0) in the paint stack, not between them. So the true paint order at any light-section pixel was: body's white (bottom) → Footer's real content (z-0) → `<main>`'s transparent box (z-10, wins the *order* but has nothing to paint) — meaning Footer showed straight through the "winning" layer the entire time. Dark sections were never affected, since `bg-ink` already gives them their own real background.

**Fix**: added `bg-paper` directly to `<main>` in `app/page.tsx`, alongside its existing `relative z-10`. Now the paint stack is body (white, bottom) → Footer (`z-0`, real content) → `<main>` (`z-10`, now *also* real, opaque white) — Footer is fully covered wherever no darker section supplies its own background, and still only becomes visible once `<main>`'s real content has scrolled past that screen position, same as before.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Re-ran the same ancestor-background-chain check at 4 scroll positions spanning light and dark sections (`1900`/Trust Signals, `4300`/near top, `6500`/mid-page, `9000`/mid-page) — every one now hits a real, opaque background (`MAIN` or a section's own `DIV`) before reaching Footer's `z-0` layer. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30) on a clean rebuild. `npm run build` clean.

### 4m · HeaderOverlayNav — exploratory alternate nav, styleguide only — 2026-08-27
Owner request: "do something similar" to `afternow.co/services/`'s nav, don't replace the existing Header, build it as a separate component, no design given ("just follow what's in this reference, keep my visual language, font size etc").

Confirmed the reference's actual behaviour live (DOM/computed-style inspection, not a guess): a sticky top bar (logo, plain inline links, CTA, a small always-visible toggle) that, on click, fades out the inline links/CTA and opens a full-screen panel *below* the still-visible top bar (logo + toggle stay put, toggle icon swaps to a close state). ### 4l · Three real-1920px-screen bugs: Hero's wide-pilot removed, two headings that broke 2-line wraps only at 1920+ — 2026-08-26
Owner tested on an actual large screen and reported two things: Hero's banner/video reading a different width than the rest of the page, and Our Services' and FAQ's headings wrapping to 3 lines instead of the 2 every other viewport already gets right.

**Hero's wide-pilot removed** (see the decision log entry above) — `hero.bannerInner`/`hero.videoWrap` drop `.hero-wide-pilot`; the class itself is deleted from `app/globals.css` entirely, not left dead. Hero now measures the same `container-p`-capped 1440px as every other section at 1920px, confirmed live.

**Our Services and FAQ's headings wrapping to 3 lines at 1920px only** — both use `text-h1`, whose own clamp token grows from 54px at 1440 to 65px at 1920 (a deliberate, existing sitewide choice — see the headline-scaling decision entry). Neither heading's column width changes at 1920 (both stay capped at `container-p`'s standard 1440px), so the same column that wraps a string to exactly 2 lines at 54px wraps it to 3 at 65px, with no more room available.

- **FAQ** (`faq.desktopHeading`, `shrink-0`, genuinely sized by its own `max-width`): raising the max-width from 474px to 500px at 1920px+ (`min-[1920px]:max-w-[500px]`) was the right fix and worked immediately — confirmed by measuring the real minimum width for 2 lines at the 1920 font size (484px), then rounding up for a small safety margin.
- **Our Services** (`ourServices.desktopHeadingWidth`, no `shrink-0`): the identical-looking fix (raising `max-w-[579px]`, even to as much as 2000px inline) did **nothing** — confirmed by live measurement that the column's actual rendered width doesn't come from its own `max-width` at all, but from flex-shrink math: `container-p`'s own content width (1280, since `container-p` itself stays capped at 1440 including its padding) minus the 221px gap minus the card column's fixed 480px, which computes to exactly 579px regardless of viewport or heading `max-width`. Since neither the gap nor the card width are safe to touch (both Figma-confirmed), the actual fix instead freezes this one heading's font-size at its 1440 value (`min-\[1920px\]:!text-[3.375rem]`, 54px) — the same 579px column that already wraps this string to 2 lines at 54px keeps doing so, it's simply never asked to grow into room that was never there.
- **A real cascade bug found applying that fix**: `min-[1920px]:text-[3.375rem]` compiled and appeared in the class list correctly, but `xl:text-h1` (from the shared `sectionHeading.heading` recipe, applied earlier in the `cx()` call) still won at a real 1920px viewport — confirmed by testing without `!important` first. This is the same same-specificity cascade-order risk the project's `max-xl:`/`xl:` rule already exists for, but here the two ranges aren't mutually exclusive (1920px is a subset of `xl`'s own range), so the fix is `!important`, not a reordering — added to the decision log so the next similar case doesn't re-debug this from scratch.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-measured via a real Playwright session at 1920×1080: Our Services and FAQ headings both confirmed at exactly 2 rendered lines (was 3 for both); Hero's container confirmed at 1440px, matching Our Services' own container width at the same viewport (was 1680px). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30). `npm run build` clean.
