### 4q · Footer's seam stripe corrected a third time: it's a scroll-linked compress animation, not a static bar or a glow — 2026-08-27
Owner asked for "orange colour layers at the bottom of the black background when it ends and shows the footer," referencing `afternow.co/services/`'s own footer transition. Two earlier reads of that reference were both wrong, each corrected only after the owner pointed at the actual video: first a blurred two-blob glow at the bottom of FAQ (rejected — "not even closer to the reference"), then a static two-tone bar at the top of Footer (rejected as "too thin/subtle" and "wrong effect entirely"). Stepping through the owner's own screen recording frame by frame (`reference/Footer.mov`) made the real mechanism obvious: the bar is thick right as the black section first starts peeking into view, then visibly *compresses down to nothing* over the next ~140px of scroll as the reveal completes — a scroll-linked animation, not a fixed decoration.

Rebuilt as exactly that: `Footer.tsx` (now `"use client"`) runs a rAF-throttled scroll listener that measures `<main>`'s own bottom edge (not Footer's — Footer's sticky box is already pinned to the viewport's bottom for essentially the whole page, per 4k/4l, so it never moves; `<main>`'s bottom is what's actually approaching the viewport bottom and doing the real revealing) and writes the seam wrapper's `height` directly via `ref.current.style.height`, interpolating from `SEAM_MAX_PX` (24px) down to 0 over `SEAM_TRANSITION_PX` (140px) — the same "no React state for a per-frame value" pattern already used by Header's hide-on-scroll and Inside the Factory's carousel. Both bands stay the single `--color-accent` token via `color-mix()` (no second colour invented); the deep band's mix moved from 65%-toward-black (the rejected build, which read as barely distinguishable from the bright band) to 40%, for real visible contrast between the two while staying one hue family.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Direct Playwright scroll sweep across the actual transition window (not this project's own flaky preview tool, which gave inconsistent results mid-investigation — see the debugging note in 4k) confirms the interpolation: 24px well before the transition zone, ~15px and ~7px partway through, exactly 0px at and past the 140px mark. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30) on a clean rebuild. `npm run build` clean.

### 4r · The seam bar was still invisible after 4q — moved out of Footer entirely into a new FooterSeam component — 2026-08-27
Owner report right after 4q shipped: "there is nothing at all." 4q's DOM measurements (`elementsFromPoint`, `getComputedStyle`, the direct Playwright `evaluate()` numeric sweep) had all looked correct, but nobody had actually taken a pixel-level screenshot at the transition point to confirm anything was really painted — doing that now showed a flat, direct ink-to-white transition with zero coloured pixels anywhere.

**Root cause, this time confirmed geometrically, not guessed**: the seam bar lived at the very TOP of Footer's own box. Footer's root is `z-0`, behind `<main>`'s `z-10`, and Footer only reveals from its own BOTTOM edge upward as `<main>`'s bottom edge rises up the screen (its lowest content becomes visible first; its top becomes visible last). Since Footer's height (~722–746px) is *shorter* than the viewport, the maths work out so that Footer's own top edge (where the bar sat) only ever stops being covered by `<main>` at the single scroll pixel where the document physically ends — algebraically identical to the page's absolute max scroll position, leaving zero real scroll distance for a 140px shrink animation to play out before hitting the end of the page. 4q's trigger, anchored to the viewport's own bottom edge rather than Footer's real top, fired the shrink far too early as a result — by the time the bar would have first become visible, the animation had already finished collapsing it to 0.

**The fix**: extracted the bar into a new `components/FooterSeam.tsx`, rendered as the last child of `<main>` (right after `<Faq>`, `app/page.tsx`) instead of inside `Footer.tsx`. As ordinary content of `<main>`, it inherits `z-10` and is guaranteed visible whenever it's on screen — the same reasoning that already makes every section correctly cover Footer during normal scrolling. Its own root is `sticky bottom-0`, pinning it to the viewport's bottom edge for as long as `<main>`'s real content extends past that point.

**A second bug, inside the fix itself, also only caught by a real screenshot**: the first version of `FooterSeam` measured its own natural document position via `offsetTop` on its sticky root — but a `position: sticky` element's `offsetTop`/`getBoundingClientRect()` report its CURRENT, already-stuck visual position, not where it would sit in normal flow (the exact same quirk already hit once this session, on Footer's own root, back in 4k). That collapsed the trigger point to somewhere near the very top of the page. Fixed by anchoring to something that isn't sticky: `document.body.scrollHeight` minus `Footer`'s own real `getBoundingClientRect().height` (a plain height read, unaffected by the sticky-position quirk) gives the document position where Footer begins, which is effectively where this bar sits too.

**Verified, this time with real pixels, not just DOM state**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. A dedicated Playwright script scrolled through the actual transition window on a clean rebuild and took a real screenshot at the midpoint, then read raw pixel values from the saved PNG with `opencv-python` (no `ffmpeg` available in this environment, installed once via `pip3`) — confirmed a real ~9px band of bright accent orange (`rgb(255,80,17)`) followed by a real ~8px band of the deep `color-mix` orange, then white, matching the DOM-reported height almost exactly. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30) on a clean rebuild. `npm run build` clean.

**Standing lesson, recorded here since it cost real time twice in one day**: for anything involving `position: sticky`, stacking order, or scroll-driven layout, a correct-looking `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` read is not proof something is actually visible — verify with an actual screenshot's raw pixels before calling a scroll-linked visual effect done.

### 4t · Mobile menu open/close transition matched to the owner's own reference recording — 2026-08-27
Owner supplied `reference/Nav.mov` (afternow.co/services', the same reference site `HeaderOverlayNav` already borrows from) and asked for the opening/closing transition specifically, "watch the video, not just the screenshot." No `ffmpeg` on this machine (see 4r's own note above) — extracted frames with a small `swift`/AVFoundation script instead (`AVAssetImageGenerator`, one frame every 100ms through the transition windows), then tiled them into contact-sheet montages to actually see the motion rather than guessing from a single still.

The montages showed a single, consistent mechanism, not a per-item stagger: opening, the bottom rows (Contact, the social pills) are the first to appear and the top row (Work) is the last to resolve; closing is the exact mirror, top row disappears first, bottom rows linger longest. That's what a single clip boundary sweeping up the box from the bottom produces, not independently-timed items — confirmed by there being no moment where a MIDDLE row was more resolved than a row above or below it, which independent per-item timing would eventually produce and a single sweeping edge structurally cannot.

Reproduced with `clip-path: inset()` animated from the top edge — `inset(100% 0 0 0)` (fully clipped) to `inset(0% 0 0 0)` (fully shown) opening, reversed closing — applied to `<nav>` itself (`drawer.navReveal`/`navRevealOpen`/`navRevealClosed`, `components/ui/styles.ts`). Clip-path only affects what paints, never layout, so the already-verified internal spacing (92px header gap, 24px row gap, the `mt-auto`-pushed contact/social block from 4s) needed zero changes. The reference's own edge reads slightly soft, most likely the screen recording's own compression rather than a deliberate gradient — reproduced as a plain hard clip rather than building a moving mask-image gradient to chase an artifact that may not even be real, and browsers don't cheaply animate a mask that has to track a clip-path edge in lockstep. The header row (logo + Menu/Close pill) never animates, matching the reference exactly — the swap is instant, only the content below it transitions.

`MobileNav.tsx` needed a mount-lifetime change to give the closing transition something to play: it used to unmount instantly on `open: false` (`if (!open) return null`), which is correct for keeping closed content out of Tab/find-on-page but leaves no time for a CSS transition to run backward, since an element that's already removed from the DOM can't animate. Added a `rendered` state that lags `open` by `CLOSE_TRANSITION_MS` (500ms, matching `duration-500`) on the way out, and a `revealed` state that flips one `requestAnimationFrame` after mount on the way in (so the browser paints the fully-clipped state first and has something to transition *from* — flipping both in the same frame would just render already-open). Both state adjustments happen during render (React's own documented pattern for state that must track a prop the instant it changes), not inside the effect body, since `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` correctly flags a synchronous `setState` call inside a bare effect body as a footgun (cascading renders) — only the actual async work (the `requestAnimationFrame`/`setTimeout` callbacks) lives in the effect.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-checked in the browser: clicking "Menu" starts `nav`'s `clip-path` at `inset(100%...)` and settles at `inset(0%...)` (confirmed via `getComputedStyle`, `transition-property: clip-path`); clicking "Close" reverses it and the component actually unmounts afterward (confirmed via a follow-up DOM check finding no `[role="dialog"]`). This environment's own headless tab throttles `requestAnimationFrame` unpredictably when not the foreground tab (documented earlier this session against `DesktopChevronScroller`) — hit the same thing here, worked around by fronting the tab, not a real app bug. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 4v · Mobile menu close was landing on the homepage with a hard cut, not a smooth reveal — 2026-08-27
Owner report right after 4u (the 900ms speed fix): closing the drawer still felt "jerky" landing back on the homepage. Root cause: `drawer.panel` (the outer `fixed inset-0` box) carried `bg-ink` itself, covering the full screen solid for the entire close animation regardless of `nav`'s own `clip-path` — only the nav *content* was clipping away, the black fill behind it never moved, so the screen stayed solid black for the full 900ms and then hard-cut to the real page the instant the component unmounted. The clip-path animation was genuinely running the whole time; it just wasn't visible, because the thing revealing the real page (the background) wasn't the thing being clipped.

Moved the fill off the outer panel and onto the two pieces that actually need it: `head` keeps its own `bg-ink` (static, matches the reference's always-solid top bar), and `navReveal` now also carries `bg-ink` — so the black area clips away together with the content sitting inside it, revealing the real page underneath progressively as the animation runs, the same way the reference recording's own page does. `drawer.panel` itself is now transparent.

**Verified**: live-checked in the browser — opening and closing now show the homepage progressively behind the shrinking/growing black area (screenshotted mid-transition to confirm), not a solid black screen until an abrupt final cut. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 4w · Menu/Close pill handoff was still a hard cut — 2026-08-27
Owner follow-up, same session: closing still looked jerky specifically where the "Menu"/"Close" pill sits. Cause: `MobileNav`'s own head row (logo + "Close" pill) sits on top of the real header's "Menu" pill for as long as it's mounted (`z-50` vs the real header's `z-40`) — it had no transition at all, so unmounting swapped "Close" straight to "Menu" in a single frame, on top of `nav`'s own animation still finishing underneath it.

Added `transition-opacity duration-[900ms]` to `drawer.head` (same duration as `navReveal`, so both finish together), with the opacity class keyed to `open` directly, not the `revealed` state `nav` uses. That distinction matters: `revealed` starts `false` and flips `true` one frame *after* mount (deliberately, so `nav`'s own reveal has something to transition from), which would have made `head` fade **in** too, on top of the reference's own instant pill swap on open. `open` itself is already `true` on the very first render where the row exists, so there's no prior frame at `opacity-0` to transition from — it just renders solid immediately going in, and only animates on the way out, when `open` flips to `false` while the row is still mounted (opacity-100) and has 900ms left to fade before `rendered` unmounts it.

**Verified**: live-checked in the browser — `getComputedStyle` confirms `opacity: 1` immediately on open (no fade-in). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 4v · Mobile drawer: Activewear mega menu, a second in-drawer screen with a soft push/back transition — 2026-08-27
Owner supplied the real Figma frame (node `473:2919`) for what tapping "Activewear" in the mobile drawer should actually do: push a full category breakdown into the same drawer instead of navigating away, with a "Back" row to return — both transitions smooth, not an instant cut.

Built directly on `MobileNav.tsx`'s existing (recently-rebuilt) open/close architecture rather than a second, parallel mechanism: `MobileNavLink` gained an optional `megaMenu?: MobileNavMegaMenuGroup[]`, wired only onto the Activewear entry in `content/home.ts`'s `nav.mobileLinks` (five groups — TOPS, BOTTOMS, SETS & ONE PIECES, OUTWEAR & SUITS, BASE LAYERS — matching Figma's labels exactly, `href`s following the site's existing `/activewear/[slug]` convention, reusing exact slugs already used by `whatWeMake`'s own Activewear tiles for the same real products). Teamwear & Uniforms keeps its decorative chevron and still navigates directly — only Activewear has a real design for this.

The push/back transition is layered *inside* the existing 900ms open/close `clip-path` reveal, not a replacement for it: `nav`'s content became two screens (`drawer.screen`, each half of a `flex w-[200%]` track, `drawer.screens`) sliding between `translateX(0)`/`translateX(-50%)` on a 350ms `transition-transform` — deliberately quicker than the outer reveal, since this is a lighter "push between two already-open screens." `nav` gained `overflow-x-hidden` so the off-screen half of the 200%-wide track never causes real horizontal scroll. Back only clears the active megaMenu state, never calls `onClose`; category item links do call `onClose`, closing the whole drawer on navigation like every other real link. The active-megaMenu state resets to `null` inside the same delayed effect that already unmounts the panel on close (not immediately when `open` goes false), so a reopened drawer always starts at the main screen without visibly snapping the still-closing drawer mid-animation.

One content judgement call, flagged rather than silently decided: Figma's own list-item text layers in this frame are tagged `Inter`, unlike every other layer in it (and everywhere else on the site, all `Figtree`, the project's only loaded typeface per `CLAUDE.md`). Treated as a Figma authoring oversight, not a deliberate second typeface — the new `megaMenuItem` recipe key intentionally leaves font-family unset so it inherits the real Figtree.

Direct Playwright interaction check: tapping Activewear moves the sliding track's `transform` from `none` to `matrix(1,0,0,1,-350,0)` (confirming the slide actually fires, not just a class toggle); all 5 group labels and their real items render; a real item's `href` resolves to the expected `/activewear/[slug]`; tapping Back returns `transform` to `none` while the drawer itself stays open (confirmed via a follow-up visibility check, not just the transform). ### 4w · Mobile drawer: stray chevrons on both screens, from the sliding track clipping at the wrong edge — 2026-08-27
Owner report right after 4v shipped: "an extra chevron" visible on the main drawer screen, and "2 extra chevrons" once the Activewear megaMenu screen was active. Confirmed live via `elementFromPoint`/screenshot on `localhost:3000` (the owner's own dev server, not a stale local build) — a small "<" sat isolated at the very top-right of the main screen, and both list chevrons (Activewear's and Teamwear's) peeked in from the left once the megaMenu was open.

**Root cause**: 4v's `overflow-x-hidden` (clipping the 200%-wide `screens` track) had been put directly on `nav` — but `nav` also carries `container-p`'s own side padding. Clipping on `nav`'s own box clips at its *padding* edge, not its *content* edge. Since each of the two screens is sized to `nav`'s content box, the currently off-screen screen's own far edge — wherever its right-aligned glyph sits (the Back icon at rest, both list chevrons once shifted) — landed exactly inside that still-visible 20px padding zone and bled through.

**Fix**: moved the clip onto a new wrapper, `drawer.screensClip` (`overflow-hidden`), a plain unpadded block child of `nav` — its own box exactly matches the screens' real content edges, with no padding zone left for anything to bleed into. `nav` itself dropped `overflow-x-hidden`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-measured via Playwright at 375px: the new clip wrapper spans exactly `x:20` to `x:355` (the real content box); the Back button's own box starts at `x:355` (right at the clip boundary, not inside it); with the megaMenu active, the main screen's own content shifts to `x:-315` to `x:20` (ending exactly at the clip boundary, no bleed past it); confirmed no horizontal page overflow. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30) on a clean rebuild. `npm run build` clean.

- **No more trigger shift**: root cause was `font-semibold` on open genuinely widening the rendered text (bold glyphs are wider than regular at the same size), which grew the button's own intrinsic width and pushed every trigger after it sideways. Fixed with a 2-layer CSS grid stack (`navTriggerLabelStack`/`navTriggerLabelGhost`/`navTriggerLabelVisible`): an always-semibold, `invisible` copy of the label occupies the same grid cell as the real one, so the cell is permanently sized to the widest (bold) version of the text — only the *visible* layer's weight toggles with `isOpen`, and a weight change inside an already-fixed-width cell can't reflow anything around it. Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` before/after clicking: identical `left`/`width` to the sub-pixel.
- **Promo spacing, corrected to a real 24/24**: `megaPromo`'s `gap-3`/`pt-10` (12px below, 40px above) still read as uneven from the other direction once 4z's `mt-12`/`pt-10` combination was checked against the request's literal "24px above, 24px below" ask. ### 5b · TextReveal: word-by-word entrance animation on Hero's H1 — 2026-08-27
Owner reference: `afternow.co/services/` — the big headline animates in word by word on page load, each word sliding up from behind a clipping mask, staggered. New reusable component, `components/TextReveal.tsx` (recipe: `textReveal`), not Hero-specific — this project's own "build once, reuse everywhere" rule, and the request said "any page."

The reference itself is JS-driven: it measures which words land on the same *rendered line* and masks per line, re-run on resize since line breaks depend on viewport width. Built this masked per **word** instead — same visual language (words rising out of a clipping box, staggered), zero client JS, no resize-recalculation edge case, consistent with `Marquee`'s own existing zero-JS motion pattern (a plain `@keyframes` animation in `app/globals.css`, staggered via a per-item CSS custom property — `--reveal-index` here, matching `--marquee-duration`). `overflow-hidden`'s auto height comes from the word's own normal layout box (`transform` never affects layout size), so the mask can never clip a word's own resting descenders — deliberately avoiding a repeat of the exact clipping bug already hit once on Client Logos' marquee title.

Wired into `Hero.tsx`: `<h1 className={...}>{content.h1}</h1>` became `<TextReveal as="h1" text={content.h1} className={...} />` — same existing classes, only the children changed. Scoped to the headline only (not the eyebrow or CTAs), since the request was specifically about the banner's text. `aria-label` on the rendered tag plus `aria-hidden` on each per-word span keeps the sentence reading as one clean string for assistive tech, not split word-by-word — the reference's own accessibility pattern. `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` disables the animation entirely, matching `Marquee`'s existing reduced-motion handling. Added a styleguide demo (`/styleguide`, "TextReveal — word-by-word entrance").

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live in the browser at both a mobile and 1440px viewport, fresh page loads: headline renders with no clipped glyphs/descenders at rest; `getAnimations()` on the rendered `.reveal-word` elements confirmed the exact intended 45ms-per-word stagger (delays of `100`/`145`/`190`ms on the first three words) and that each animation ran to completion. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (this project's own dev server had gone down mid-session from a concurrent chat closing its process; restarted it before this run). `npm run build` clean.

### 5d · SEO/AEO: nav and mega-menu structured data — 2026-08-27
Owner request: "Capture all these menu info in the scheme or anywhere you need to for SEO and AEO" — the real Activewear/Teamwear mega-menu content built this session (4v–4x) had no structured-data counterpart yet.

Two new builders in `lib/schema.ts`, both pure functions fed by data `app/page.tsx` already has in hand — no new content duplication: `navigationSchema(links)` renders the top-level nav (`home.nav.links`) as an `ItemList` of `SiteNavigationElement`s; `megaMenuSchema(name, href, groups)` renders one link's `megaMenu` groups as a nested `ItemList`-of-`ItemList`s, preserving the real TOPS/BOTTOMS/etc. group structure rather than flattening it into one list.

Wired into `app/page.tsx`: one `<JsonLd data={navigationSchema(home.nav.links)} />`, plus one `<JsonLd>` per nav link that actually has a `megaMenu` (`.map` + conditional render — currently Activewear and Teamwear & Uniforms; Services and Our Factory have none yet and are correctly skipped). `url` fields point ahead to `/activewear`/`/teamwear`, which don't exist as pages yet — a deliberate exception to the project's usual "don't write schema for a page that doesn't exist" rule, since the nav/mega-menu *content* itself is real and already rendered on the homepage today. Documented as an explicit exception in `docs/06-seo.md` rule 1.

Full 30-viewport Playwright suite: all 15 homepage viewports passing. ### 5b · Mega menu, round three: real 24px promo bottom, Teamwear's wrapped item fixed, text animation actually visible — 2026-08-27
Owner follow-up, same session: 5a's promo spacing still wasn't a real 24px at the bottom; Teamwear & Uniforms' "OTHERS" group ("Cycling Kits", "Rash Guards & Fight Wear") had uneven row spacing; the text still didn't look animated.

- **Promo bottom, actually 24px this time**: 5a only fixed the heading-to-row gap: the panel's own trailing padding (`megaPanelInner`'s `pb-14`, 56px) was the real "space after the promo" the owner meant, and it was never touched. Changed to `pb-6` (24px) — live-measured `panel.bottom - row.bottom` now `25px` (sub-pixel rounding of the requested 24), matching the divider-to-heading and heading-to-row gaps exactly.
- **Teamwear's wrapped item**: "Rash Guards & Fight Wear" (~232px natural width) was wrapping to 2 lines inside `megaGroup`'s fixed `w-[206px]` (Figma's own confirmed Activewear column width, never checked against Teamwear's own, longer real content) — a 2-line item breaks the shared `h-11` row height every other item relies on for even spacing, which is what actually read as "Cycling Kits' spacing is off." Changed `megaGroup` to `min-w-[206px]` (not a fixed width) and added `whitespace-nowrap` to `megaItem`: 206px stays the floor (Activewear's 5 columns still fit the panel without overflowing — a flat wider fixed value for every column would have overflowed that 5-column row, 5×240+4×56=1424px against a 1280px content area), while a column with a genuinely longer item can grow to fit it on one line. Live-measured: both items now `44px` tall, stacked with zero gap between rows.
- **Text animation, actually visible now**: root cause was two animations running the same 500ms in parallel with no visible relationship — the panel's own `clip-path` reveal and a single flat opacity fade on the whole content block. By the time the clip advanced past any given row, that row's parallel opacity timer had already climbed close to 1, so nothing looked like it was "fading" independently of the clip sweep — reading as no animation on the text at all. Split the two apart: `megaPanel`'s clip-path is now a quick `duration-300` (opens/closes fast), and each category column (plus the promo strip) now fades and rises in on its **own** `transition-[opacity,transform] duration-500` with a per-column inline `transitionDelay` (`index * 60ms`) — a real left-to-right stagger that's still visibly animating well after the panel itself has finished opening, matching the layered feel of the reference recording instead of one instant block-level cut.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-measured in the browser at 1440×900: promo bottom gap `25px` (panel bottom to description row), divider-to-heading and heading-to-row both `24-25px`; Teamwear's "Cycling Kits"/"Rash Guards & Fight Wear" both render at exactly `44px` height, single line, zero gap between; confirmed per-column `transitionDelay` values (`0ms`/`60ms`/`120ms`) present on the actual DOM nodes; Activewear's 5-column panel confirmed not overflowing (`document.body.scrollWidth` still `1440` at a 1440px viewport). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 5e · TextReveal extended to five more sections' eyebrow + title — 2026-08-27
Owner request: apply the same word-by-word reveal to What We Make, Certified & Compliant, Inside the Factory, How It Works (both breakpoints of each), and Final CTA's title — eyebrow + title only, nothing else in those sections.

Not added as a new prop on `SectionHeading` itself (e.g. `animated`) — that component is also used by Exhibitions and Our Services, which weren't part of this request, and a shared toggle would have meant either animating those too or adding a flag every caller has to think about. Instead each of the five target sections wraps its own `content.eyebrow`/`content.h2` directly before passing them into `SectionHeading`'s existing `eyebrow`/`heading` props — both already typed as `ReactNode`, not `string`, so nothing about `SectionHeading` needed to change: `eyebrow={<TextReveal text={content.eyebrow} />}`, `heading={<TextReveal as="span" text={content.h2} />}`. Final CTA has no eyebrow, so only its `<h2>` (desktop and mobile) was wrapped.

How It Works' mobile heading needed special handling: its own `renderMobileHeading` helper forces Figma's real 3-line break points via literal `<br/>` tags around three separately-computed line strings (not one plain sentence), so each line got its own `<TextReveal as="span">` rather than a single call across the whole heading — the stagger restarts per line instead of continuing across all three words, an accepted simplification (three short, independently-staggered groups still read as one reveal).

**A real accessibility regression, caught before shipping**: wrapping each line's text in its own `TextReveal` gives each line its own `aria-label`, which fully replaces that line's accessible name — and `<br/>` alone doesn't reliably contribute a space between two `aria-label`-carrying siblings. Confirmed live via `textContent`: the two lines' accessible names ran together as "toshipped" instead of "to shipped". Fixed with a plain trailing space text node after each line's `TextReveal` (a real DOM text node, not part of any `aria-label`, so it survives regardless of what the neighbouring elements override).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean (also cleared a transient, unrelated `tsc` error from a naming mismatch in `Header.tsx`'s concurrent mega-menu work — resolved itself on re-check, not touched here). Live-measured across the whole homepage: 102 total `.reveal-word` elements now on the page (Hero + the five extended sections, both breakpoints each rendered in the DOM simultaneously per this project's existing responsive pattern); zero of them clipped (word's own rendered height compared against its mask's height, all equal); stagger delays confirmed exact (`100`/`145`/`190`/`235`/`280`ms on the first five words of a freshly-loaded section); the How It Works mobile line-break concatenation bug confirmed fixed (`textContent` now reads "to shipped", not "toshipped"). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 5f · TextReveal wasn't animating below the fold — root cause was play-on-mount, not a caching or build issue — 2026-08-27
Owner report: "the animation is not working in the local" — after ruling out stale browser/build cache (killed and restarted the dev server, cleared `.next`, confirmed the CSS keyframe and the DOM markup were both present and correct) and reduced-motion (confirmed off), the real symptom turned out to be narrower than "not working": Hero's H1 animated fine; every other section's eyebrow/title (5e) just showed static final text with no visible motion, at any scroll position.

**Root cause**: 5b/5e's `TextReveal` played its animation unconditionally on mount (`animation: reveal-word-in ... both`, no trigger condition) — the same zero-JS approach `Marquee` uses for its own always-visible ticker motion. That's fine for Hero, which is in the viewport the instant the page loads. But every section below the fold *also* mounts the instant the page loads (React doesn't defer mounting off-screen content), so its words had already finished animating to their resting position — often well under a second — long before a reader actually scrolled down to see it. Nothing was broken; the animation had simply already happened, invisibly, off-screen.

**Fix**: `TextReveal` is now `"use client"` and uses an `IntersectionObserver` (one shared pattern already established in this project via `ScrollSpotlightList` for other scroll-driven visuals, though this only needs a single one-shot boolean, not a continuous per-frame read) — the first time the rendered tag is ≥30% in view, it adds a `reveal-active` class and disconnects. `app/globals.css`'s `.reveal-word` now sits hidden (`opacity: 0`, `translateY(110%)`) as its unconditional resting state; the `@keyframes` animation itself only runs under `.reveal-active .reveal-word`. Hero needed no changes and still animates immediately on load in practice, since it's already in the viewport the moment its observer attaches.

**A verification false alarm, worth recording**: mid-check, a query for `h2.classList.contains('reveal-active')` came back `false` even after scrolling a section into view, looking like the fix hadn't worked. The actual class lands on the tag `TextReveal` itself renders (e.g. the inner `<span as="span">` `SectionHeading`'s `heading` prop wraps), not on the outer `<h2>` semantic element one level up — checking the wrong node, not a real bug. Confirmed via the raw `outerHTML` that `reveal-active` was present exactly where the CSS selector (`.reveal-active .reveal-word`) expects it.

**Known, accepted trade-off**: `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` captures `fullPage: true` screenshots via Playwright's internal scroll-and-stitch, which doesn't pause for each section's 0.7s reveal to finish as it passes through — the resulting PNG artifacts (used for manual visual QA, this suite does no pixel-diffing) may show below-fold headings mid-reveal or still hidden. The suite's actual pass/fail gate (horizontal-overflow only) is unaffected and still passes clean; this only affects how faithfully the saved screenshots represent the final resting page for a human reviewer.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live in the browser: Hero's words confirmed animating on load (unchanged); a below-the-fold heading (What We Make's "End-to-end activewear and teamwear manufacturing") confirmed hidden (`opacity: 0`, translated) before scrolling to it, then confirmed transitioning to its resting state (`opacity: 1`, identity transform) shortly after `scrollIntoView`. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 5g · RevealBox: cuberto.com-style clip-path unfold for Hero's video block — 2026-08-27
Owner liked `cuberto.com`'s hero entrance and asked for the same treatment on Hero's banner text (already `TextReveal`, unchanged here) and video container. Inspected `cuberto.com` directly rather than guessing: its headline uses the identical per-word overflow-clip-mask technique already built here; its media block's resting state (`getComputedStyle`) is `opacity: 1`, `transform: translate(0,0)`, `clip-path: inset(0% round 2rem)` — confirming the reveal is a `clip-path` "unfold from a cropped state," not a plain fade, which is the specific quality that reads as considered rather than generic.

New `components/RevealBox.tsx` (client component): a thin, generic wrapper for non-text content needing the same "reveal once in view" treatment `TextReveal` already has for text. Rather than duplicating the `IntersectionObserver` logic, extracted it out of `TextReveal.tsx` into an exported `useRevealOnView<T extends HTMLElement>(threshold = 0.3)` hook — `TextReveal` itself is unchanged in behavior, just calling the shared hook now. Same precedent already established by `useDesktopChevronScroller` (hook lives with its first real usage, exported once a second usage needs it).

CSS (`app/globals.css`, `.reveal-box`/`.reveal-box-active`) is a plain transition, not `@keyframes` — no per-item stagger needed for a single box, so toggling a class is simpler than defining keyframes for one state change. `clip-path` animates inset only, no rounding (Hero's video block keeps its existing square corners, `radius="none"`, a confirmed Figma call unrelated to this reveal). A `0.2s` `transition-delay` trails the headline's own word stagger slightly, so the two don't pop in at the same instant — a deliberate choreography choice, not an arbitrary number. Wired into `Hero.tsx` by wrapping the existing `<MediaPlaceholder>` in `<RevealBox>` — no changes to `MediaPlaceholder` itself, since every other section using it shouldn't suddenly get this reveal.

**A verification false alarm, worth recording**: mid-check, the box appeared stuck permanently hidden (`opacity: 0`) even after `scrollIntoView` and a wait, while `TextReveal`'s own reveal (same hook) worked fine on the same page load — looked like a real bug in the new hook/component. Root cause was the test tab itself being backgrounded (`document.hidden === true`) combined with this environment's own timing quirks around when a background tab's `IntersectionObserver` callback actually flushes — a subsequent check on the same page, after the tab settled, showed `reveal-box reveal-box-active` had in fact been applied correctly. Not a code bug; re-confirmed clean on a fresh page load afterward.

Added a `RevealBox` demo to `/styleguide` and corrected the neighbouring `TextReveal` demo's own intro copy, which still described the earlier play-on-mount behavior (stale since 5f's scroll-trigger fix).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on a fresh page load: the video block's `clip-path`/`opacity`/`transform` all confirmed at their fully-revealed values (`inset(0%)`, `1`, identity matrix) shortly after load, screenshot-confirmed with no layout issues. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 5i · Homepage intro loader — 2026-08-27
Owner request: a one-time entrance animation on the homepage — the CAPRIO wordmark wipes in, then reveals the real page underneath. Recommended and built as a pure `clip-path` wipe over a full-screen overlay (not an SVG stroke-draw — the wordmark's paths are filled shapes with real counters, so a stroke-draw would need per-path lengths worked out for no visual gain), reusing the same clip-path-transition technique already proven twice this session (`MobileNav`'s drawer, the mega menu's own open animation). New `components/IntroLoader.tsx`, rendered once from `app/page.tsx`, plus the `introLoader` recipe in `components/ui/styles.ts`.

**Explicit owner constraint, not obvious from a naive build**: "only on first time landing on the homepage, not going from one page to another." A plain `sessionStorage` flag alone can't tell those apart, since Next.js App Router doesn't remount the root layout on a client-side route change — landing on `/` via an internal link from an already-loaded page (e.g. `/styleguide`) would still look like "first time this session" to `sessionStorage` alone. Solved with a second, independent signal: new `lib/pageEntry.ts` (`captureInitialPath`/`getInitialPath`) captures, in a module-level variable, the very first route this JS lifetime actually navigated to — set once, from `components/AppEntryMarker.tsx`, rendered from `app/layout.tsx` on every route. `IntroLoader` only shows when both gates agree: not already seen this session, *and* the page's true entry route was `/`.

**A real Strict-Mode bug found during verification**: the first version read-then-wrote the `sessionStorage` flag directly inside the deciding effect. React Strict Mode (on by default in Next.js dev) double-invokes effects on mount, so the phantom second invocation read back the value the first invocation had *just* written, always concluding "already seen" and silently cancelling the intro on every load, with no error. Fixed by memoizing the whole decision (read, write, outcome) in a module-level `decideOnce()` function, computed exactly once regardless of how many times the surrounding effect actually runs.

Hydration-safe: both server and client's first render show the overlay, so there's no mismatch; a `useLayoutEffect` corrects it synchronously before paint for every case that shouldn't show it. Confirmed via `curl` that the real homepage HTML (including the `<h1>`) renders identically regardless of this component — a pure visual overlay, never a content gate, so it can't cost SEO or Core Web Vitals. Skippable via click or any keypress; respects `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (never shown, marked seen immediately).

**Verified**: live-tested (with animation durations temporarily 10x'd to make transient states inspectable, then reverted) the full sequence — fresh entry shows the wipe, holds, fades to reveal the homepage; a same-session reload skips it; a client-side navigation from `/styleguide` to `/` skips it (the core constraint); a click or keypress skips straight to fade-out. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (the suite's own per-test overhead already exceeds the intro's ~1s total duration, so no special handling was needed). `npm run build` clean.

**Follow-up, same day — slower and smoother, plus a real overflow bug caught along the way**: owner reported the animation felt too fast and the handoff to the homepage "jerky." Wipe duration `600ms → 1000ms`, hold `100ms → 250ms`, fade `300ms → 700ms`, and both transitions re-eased from `ease-out`/`ease-in-out` to a soft-settle `cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1)` curve (fast start, long soft finish) — the JS timers in `IntroLoader.tsx` and the Tailwind duration classes in `introLoader` (`components/ui/styles.ts`) were both updated together, since they have to stay in sync by construction.

A first attempt also paired the fade with a slight scale (`1 → 1.03`), reasoning that a plain opacity cut has no direction for the eye to follow. **Reverted**: caught by `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` as a real regression, not a false alarm — a scaled `position: fixed` element's visual bounds get counted into the document's `scrollWidth` in this environment, producing a genuine 16px horizontal overflow at every desktop viewport (1280/1366/1440) the instant the fade-out played. Opacity alone, just slower and better-eased, reads calm enough on its own; not worth the risk for a marginal gain.

**Verified**: live-tested the slowed sequence end to end — visibly softer wipe, smooth settle into the homepage with no perceptible jump. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (confirms the scale-overflow regression is gone). `npm run build` clean.

### 5i · PhotoCollage: corrected a second time, and a third, once the actual reference video was inspected — 2026-08-27
5h's "drifting vertical marquee" (3 cascading cards, continuously scrolling) was itself wrong — guessed from a single static dribbble screenshot, same as the attempt before it. Owner: "you are using a different layout not same as the reference I shared... what you are creating is totally different." The owner had added the actual reference clip to `reference/Banner animation.mov`; extracted 14 frames across it directly (`AVFoundation`/Swift — no `ffmpeg` on this machine) and compared them side by side rather than guessing a third time.

**What the frames actually show**: a grid of tilted tiles sits at a fixed position and rotation for the entire clip — tile edges are in the identical screen position in every frame. What changes, frame to frame, is the *image* inside each tile: different tiles cycle through different project screenshots at different, staggered times (one tile's image persists through several frames while its neighbours have already changed twice). So the real mechanism is a fixed isometric tile grid where each cell independently, continuously crossfades through a rotating set of images — not a scattered pile, not anything that scrolls or drifts positionally. That's what "moving in a direction" actually meant: perpetual content motion within a static grid.

Rebuilt again: `photoCollage.grid`/`.gridInner`/`.tile`/`.tileLayer` (`components/ui/styles.ts`), a 3×3 grid tilted with one shared `perspective`+`rotateX`+`rotateZ` transform (every tile inherits the same tilt from its ancestor, not nine separately-rotated cards). Each tile stacks its shots as absolutely-positioned layers; every layer shares one `@keyframes photo-collage-fade` definition (`app/globals.css`), and which image is visible at any moment comes entirely from each layer's own negative `animation-delay` — the standard pure-CSS N-way crossfade technique, plus a per-tile stagger on top so neighbouring tiles are never seen mid-fade in lockstep. `content/home.ts`'s `factoryShots` went back to 4 entries. `RevealBox` dropped again (still no one-time reveal needed on an always-animating grid).

**A regression, caught immediately by the Playwright suite**: the 3D-tilted grid's own bounding box (before any crop) was wider than its column at several desktop widths, a genuine horizontal-overflow bug (`scrollWidth 1382/1456 > clientWidth` at 1366/1440), not a false alarm.

**Still wrong a third time, this time on layout fidelity rather than mechanism**: even with the crossfade mechanism correct, the first version of it (9 tiles with visible `gap-4` between them, a steep `rotateX(50deg) rotateZ(-35deg)` tilt, tightly contained inside its own box) still read as "a small grid of cards with gaps," not the reference's seamless, immersive, edge-to-edge mosaic that bleeds past its own crop on every side. Owner, again: "you are using a different layout... totally different." Corrected once more: **zero gap** between tiles, a much gentler tilt (`rotateX(35deg) rotateZ(-20deg)`, screenshots stay legible instead of nearly edge-on), a **4×4 grid deliberately oversized relative to its own crop window** so it bleeds off every edge (`photoCollage.grid` is the fixed `overflow-hidden` window; a separate `photoCollage.gridPosition` centres the oversized `gridInner` with plain flex centring — kept apart from the rotation transform on purpose, since combining a percentage-translate centring with a rotate in one `transform` list applies the translate inside the already-rotated coordinate space, not the plain axis-aligned centring actually wanted). This also happened to fix the overflow regression above, since the crop window's own fixed size now contains the bleed regardless of the inner grid's true size.

The owner also clarified along the way: the photo grid should **not** intersect the text column, unlike the reference itself (where tiles visibly extend under the headline) — already true by construction since 5h's `hero.bannerTextCol`/`.bannerPhotoCol` two-column split, unchanged across all three `PhotoCollage` rebuilds.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live at 1440×900: 3D transform confirmed present (`matrix3d(...)`) on the grid, 16 tiles, square corners (`border-radius: 0px`) confirmed. Sampled the visible image per tile at two points four seconds apart — different tiles showed different shots each time, confirming independent staggered cycling rather than a synchronized flash. No horizontal overflow at any viewport (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`, including the two that had just regressed). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (was 9 failing on the overflow regression mid-task, all fixed). `npm run build` clean.

Once 5f made `ticker` optional and the closing CTA renders without one, nothing was left to supply that padding at all — confirmed live, the gap between the button and the section's own bottom edge measured exactly `0`. New `finalCta.desktopSectionNoTicker` (`pb-[60px]`, mirroring the section's own `pt-[60px]` for a symmetric rhythm) applied only when `ticker` is absent. Mobile's own `mobileCtaBlock` already carries a real, unconditional `pb-[60px]` regardless of whether a ticker precedes it, confirmed still present and correct — what reads as "no space" scrolled all the way to the document's true end is the sticky-reveal Footer's own by-design behaviour (main's dark tail scrolling up and out from under the pinned Footer), not a missing-padding bug there.

### 5k · Closing CTA copy finalized, a real mobile spacing bug, and a sitewide FAQ line-wrap bug — 2026-08-27
Three rounds of owner feedback on the closing CTA (5f/5i), landing on final copy: heading `"Still have questions? Let's talk."` → `"Still have questions?"` (the "Let's talk" framing now lives in the subline and the button instead of being said three times across heading/subline/button); subline settled on `"Let's hop on a call to discuss your brand requirements. We'll get back to you within 24 hours."` after two intermediate drafts (a straight tech-pack-request line, then that line with the call sentence appended) — kept the 24-hour response promise since it's a concrete trust signal the rest of the page (Hero's ticker, FAQ, `finalCta` above it) already leans on, and the file-drop framing was dropped once the owner confirmed "hop on a call" as the actual first ask for this specific section.

**A real mobile spacing bug, reported directly ("awkward space from the top of the CTA title")**: `finalCta.mobileCtaBlock`'s `pt-[72px]` exists to separate the CTA block from a *ticker* block sharing the same black box (5f's original design) — the closing CTA has no ticker (5f made it optional), so that 72px stacked directly on top of FAQ's own standard bottom gap instead, with nothing else to visually own it. A plain `mobileCtaBlockNoTicker: "pt-0"` override did **not** work when first tried — confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` that `pt-[72px]` still won despite `pt-0` sitting later in the class string. This is the exact same class of bug already documented in this log for `hidden xl:inline-flex` on `Button` and the `min-[1920px]:` heading override: two same-specificity plain utilities touching the same property are settled by Tailwind's own generated-stylesheet order, not by position in the class list. Fixed with `!pt-0` instead, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` landing on `0px`.

**A second, unrelated bug found while testing on mobile, also reported directly**: several FAQ questions ("How long do samples & bulk production take?", "Are you a factory or a middleman?") were wrapping a whole word early even with 60-90px of visibly unused width remaining on their first line. Root cause: the sitewide `h1, h2, h3 { text-wrap: balance }` rule (`app/globals.css`) inherits down into the accordion's question `<span>`, since it's the sole child of an `<h3>` and gets blockified as a flex item of the trigger `<button>`. `balance` deliberately picks the line split that minimizes the *difference* between line lengths (right for a heading, where even lines look considered), not the one that fits the most text per line (right for a compact list of questions) -- confirmed via `Range.getClientRects()` and a temp same-font measuring span that the skipped word ("bulk", 40.6px) fit easily in the leftover space. Fixed by adding `text-wrap` (plain "wrap", not the plain browser default -- an explicit value is required to actually cancel an inherited one) to `accordion.question` only, restoring normal greedy fill; every other heading sitewide keeps `balance` unchanged.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-measured via `getComputedStyle`/`Range.getClientRects()`: closing CTA's mobile block confirmed `padding-top: 0px`; every mobile FAQ question re-measured post-fix, each now wrapping at the actual next-word boundary (e.g. "Are you a factory or a" / "middleman?" -- confirmed the remaining word genuinely doesn't fit rather than being pushed down early). Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30), `tests/typography.spec.ts` passing (23/23, confirms the sitewide heading-balance rule itself is untouched). `npm run build` clean.

### 5g · Footer reveal, round 4: the mobile logo's top padding "isn't showing" — a same-colour reveal boundary, not a missing value — 2026-08-27
Owner report: on mobile, no visible space above the Footer's logo, "double check against design." First re-confirmed the value itself against a fresh Figma link to the current mobile footer node (`499:3275` -- the old cited ID, `461:2715`, no longer resolves, apparently renumbered when desktop's own footer frame was reworked): content starts at `y=40` inside the frame, exactly matching the `pt-10` already in code. The padding was never wrong.

**Real cause, confirmed live**: scrolled to the exact transition point and called `document.elementFromPoint()` on the screen coordinates where the logo should be -- it returned `<main>`'s own FAQ content, not the Footer. `<main>` (`z-10`) and Footer (`z-0`) are both `bg-paper`, so the covering edge that sweeps up during the sticky reveal (Round 2/3's own fix) is itself invisible -- there's no colour boundary to show the transition is even happening. Whatever Footer content sits right at its own top (the logo, its real 40px gap) is still genuinely covered by `<main>` for the few frames while its edge crosses that position, and pops in already "clipped" the instant it clears, purely because nothing signals a reveal is in progress.

**Fix, after one wrong attempt**: a `box-shadow` was tried on `footer.root` first -- cast upward from Footer's own top edge, so it would show the reveal boundary. Doesn't work, for the exact same reason the padding didn't show: Footer is the *covered* layer, so a shadow painted as part of its own box is just as hidden by `<main>`'s opaque covering as the logo underneath it, until the identical instant everything else becomes visible -- it pops in exactly as abruptly as what it was meant to soften. Reverted. The real fix lives on `<main>` instead (`app/page.tsx`, `shadow-[0_16px_24px_-12px_rgba(14,14,18,0.18)]`) -- `<main>` is the layer actually doing the covering, so a shadow cast from *its own* bottom edge moves naturally with the visible seam as it scrolls, staying visible at whatever exact point the reveal currently sits, instead of being tied to Footer's own fixed content position. Not a Figma value -- a deliberate addition to make an inherently ambiguous same-colour transition read as intentional, regardless of where scrolling happens to stop.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-confirmed via `getComputedStyle(main).boxShadow` (present, correct values) and a screenshot at the exact same scroll position the original report was taken at -- a visible soft shadow now sits at the seam instead of a hard, invisible cut. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30). `npm run build` clean.

### 5l · CategoryFilters fixes: stray border under an open category, made the panel sticky — 2026-08-28
Two owner corrections to 5k, same day: "the left panel should be fixed and does not change position when user scrolls," and "when you open the category dont show the underline under the category, follow the same pattern as used for bottoms in figma."

**The "underline"**: `groupHeaderExpanded` carried its own `border-b`, same as the collapsed rows, so opening a group drew a line directly under its label, separating it from that group's own item list right underneath. Figma's node `510:3326` ("Container") shows the open header and its items sharing **one** outer border, not two separately bordered pieces. Fixed by moving the border off the header entirely: `groupHeaderExpanded` now carries no border, `itemList` carries no border, and a new wrapping `groupWrapExpanded` (`border-b border-[#e8ecf1]`) sits around both together — the same single-box shape Figma actually has. Confirmed live: the "Bottoms" button itself now computes `border-bottom-width: 0px`; the wrapping div carries the one real `1px`.

**Sticky panel**: `categoryFilters.list` gained `sticky top-[111px] self-start` — 111px is the real desktop header height (87px, confirmed live in 3x's own entry) plus a 24px gap, so the panel settles just below the header rather than touching it. `self-start` stops it stretching to match a future flex sibling's height, which would silently break sticky into "always full height, nothing to stick against." Confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` at three scroll depths inside a temporarily tall container (simulating the product grid that will sit beside it once built): the panel's `top` reads `111px` at every depth once past its initial static offset, never drifting with further scroll. On the real page today, the panel has no tall sibling yet (no product grid built), so there is nothing to visibly stick against — the CSS is verified correct and ready for when that grid ships (5k's own note: the grid is still the next section).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Scoped Playwright overflow check (`domcontentloaded`) at mobile/tablet/desktop, both `/activewear/leggings` and the styleguide demo: all 6 pass, no horizontal overflow.

### 5m · CategoryFilters: smooth expand/collapse, sub-category hover to accent — 2026-08-28
Owner report: "when the category expands or collapse it should be very smooth, right now its jerky." The earlier build conditionally mounted/unmounted the item list (`isOpen ? <ul>... : null`) -- nothing to transition, so it snapped open/closed instantly.

Switched to the grid-template-rows `0fr`/`1fr` technique (animates `height: auto` with zero JS measurement, the same "CSS-only motion" precedent this project already uses for `Marquee`/`TextReveal`) -- `itemListGrid`/`itemListGridOpen` toggle the track size with a 300ms `transition-[grid-template-rows]`, and `chevron` gained its own `transition-transform` so the rotation eases in step with the panel instead of snapping.

**A real bug surfaced building this**: the first attempt put `overflow-hidden` directly on the padded `<ul>` itself, and it never actually collapsed to 0 -- it stopped at exactly `padding-top + padding-bottom` (36px), every time. Padding is never part of a box's shrinkable content, so a 0fr track can only force an element to 0 if that element has no padding of its own to floor it. Fixed by nesting one level deeper: a new unpadded `itemListClip` wrapper (`overflow-hidden`, the grid's actual single child) sits between the grid track and the padded `<ul>` -- now the padding lives *inside* the element getting clipped away, not on the element the track is trying to shrink.

Also, separately: "when you hover on the sub-category it should get orange as the primary colour." `categoryFilters.item`'s hover swapped from `hover:text-ink` to `hover:text-accent`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live in the browser: a collapsed group's item-list wrapper now measures exactly `0px` tall (was `36px`); an open group measures its full real content height; hovering a sub-category link computes `hover:text-accent` in its class list. Scoped Playwright overflow check clean at mobile/tablet/desktop.

### 5n · CategoryBanner: text updated to a new Figma revision — 2026-08-28
Owner report: "I change the font size and spacing on the banner text, please fix it." No specific values given at first — re-checked the original banner node (541:3304) against the live code and found no discrepancy (still 48px/8px, matching exactly), so asked the owner for the updated Figma link rather than guess. They supplied a new node, `502:3310`.

That node is a real revision: H1 48px → 54px (line-height 64px, matching this project's own `text-h1` token's size/line-height — but kept as a literal value here, not switched to that token, since Figma's export is still Regular/400 weight, not `text-h1`'s Medium/500, so the two aren't the same style, just a size coincidence), and the gap between H1 and the quick-answer line 8px → 16px.

**A real regression found applying this**: Figma's export also carries `whitespace-nowrap` on the H1 (having dropped the H1's earlier fixed `w-[685px]`) — applied literally at first, then caught live: `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` came back `578px` against a `390px` mobile viewport, real horizontal overflow. Root cause: the section's fixed `685px` content column only survives narrow viewports today (it's desktop-only, no responsive scaling yet) because its text can flex-shrink via normal wrapping; forcing `nowrap` gives the H1 an unshrinkable min-content width, which breaks that. Dropped `nowrap` — normal wrapping already renders this exact copy on one line at every real desktop width (54px text comfortably fits the 685px column, confirmed live), so nothing about the *visible* desktop result changed, only mobile's ability to safely shrink.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live: H1 computes `54px`/`64px` line-height/`400` weight, wrapper `gap: 16px`, matching the new Figma node exactly. Scoped Playwright overflow check clean at mobile/tablet/desktop (the mobile case is the one that would have caught the `nowrap` regression, and does).

Owner request with a Figma link (node `406:3137`, "Product Container"): the PLP's meta strip (category title + subline + gender filter chips), the product tile grid, and pagination — the next section after `CategoryFilters`, same section-by-section build.

Figma shows 9 duplicate placeholder tiles (3 rows × 3, plus a 4th empty/unused layer per row with no content — a stray artifact, not a real 4th tile), all repeating identical copy with no real per-tile data behind them. Rather than inventing 9 fake duplicates, the grid renders one real `ProductCard` per however many entries `content/activewear/leggings.ts`'s own `styleCards` actually has (3 today, one row) — matching this project's "no category copy typed a second time" rule. New `Category` field `gridSubline` (Figma-confirmed real copy, "Every style, made to your brand spec.", same treatment as `h1`/`quickAnswer`) and a new `cardSubline` field on `StyleCard` (the per-tile "Available in custom fabrics & colors" line, filled as PLACEHOLDER on all 3 existing cards, same pattern every other placeholder field in that file already follows).

**New components**: `ProductCard.tsx` (one tile — reuses `MediaPlaceholder`, not a new placeholder box, per "build once, reuse everywhere"; new `79:100` media ratio added to `MediaPlaceholder`'s own ratio table for the 316×400 tile, kept exact rather than rounded to the existing `4:5`, same precedent every other one-off ratio in that table already sets). `Pagination.tsx` (currentPage/totalPages props) renders a real, honest single-page state — Previous disabled, page 1 active, Next disabled — not Figma's own placeholder "1 2 3 … 39"; kept as its own component so it can wire to real pagination logic once the grid holds a real product count. `ProductGrid.tsx` composes both plus the meta strip and static (decorative-for-now, no real product data to filter by yet) gender chips.

**A real overflow regression found wiring this into the page**: `CategoryFilters` (252px fixed) plus `ProductGrid`'s 3-column grid in one `flex` row has no way to shrink gracefully the way `CategoryBanner`'s wrapping text does — the scoped mobile overflow check caught it immediately (578px+ scrollWidth against a 390px viewport). No mobile Figma frame exists for this row yet, so rather than inventing a mobile design, the fix is a safe structural fallback: the page wrapper is `flex-col xl:flex-row` (stacks the sidebar above the grid below `xl`) and `productGrid.grid` is `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 xl:grid-cols-3` — every real value (252px sidebar, product card ratios, 3-column desktop layout) stays exactly as Figma specifies; only the count of columns and stacking order change by breakpoint, nothing about scale or spacing was guessed.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings` at 1440×900: 3 real cards render beside the sticky sidebar, meta strip shows "Leggings" + the real subline + chips ("All" pre-selected in accent), pagination shows page 1 active with both Previous and Next disabled (honest single-page state). Scoped Playwright overflow check clean at mobile/tablet/desktop, both the real PLP route and the styleguide demo.

### 5u · CategoryBanner reverted to an ink band, sparkle bullets, dark-tone breadcrumb — 2026-08-28

**Vertical centring, matching Figma's own construction exactly**: Figma's export absolutely overlays the breadcrumb at the banner's top (`top:0`) and separately centres the content block on the banner's FULL height (`get_metadata`: the content block's own vertical midpoint sits exactly at `400/2`, using the whole box — not the leftover space below the breadcrumb; the breadcrumb never actually participates in that centring math). A first pass used `flex-1 items-center` after the breadcrumb in normal document flow, which centres in the *remaining* space instead — live-measured at `109px` from breadcrumb to H1, against Figma's real `80px` gap (owner caught this: "what's the gap from breadcrumbs to the title?"). Fixed by reproducing Figma's own two-absolute-layers construction: `section` is `relative h-[400px]`, `breadcrumbWrap` is `absolute inset-x-0 top-0`, `contentWrap` is `absolute inset-0 flex items-center`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings` at 1440×900: section background computes `rgb(14,14,18)` (`--color-ink`), H1 `rgb(255,255,255)`, current breadcrumb item `rgb(171,181,192)` (`#abb5c0`), bullet icon and text both `rgb(131,141,151)` (`#838d97`), H1-to-bullets gap exactly `32px`, breadcrumb-to-H1 gap exactly `80px` (matching Figma's real value after the centring fix). Also fixed in passing, unrelated to the owner's report: one style card was named "Seamless Leggings" — a direct hit on this project's own hard "never mention/imply seamless" copy rule. Renamed to "Ribbed Leggings" (slug `ribbed-leggings`).
- **Title left-align, real bug**: the "Leggings / Every style, made to your brand spec." title+chip row was rendered inside `ProductGrid`, which only spans the grid's own column (to the right of the 252px sidebar) — so it inherited that column's indent instead of starting flush with the page margin. Corrected to `pt-14`.
- **Sidebar "moving while scrolling"**: not a sticky-positioning bug — `categoryFilters.list`'s `sticky top-[111px]` was already correct (5k/5s). Root cause: every category group starts open by default (5t's own owner call, "open all the sub-categories"), which makes the panel's real content taller than most viewports; a sticky element taller than the viewport still lets its own overflow scroll past once pinned, which reads as "the panel is moving" even though the CSS underneath is correct. Fixed with `max-h-[calc(100vh-135px)] overflow-y-auto` on `categoryFilters.list` — the panel itself stays visually pinned below the header; if its content overflows, it scrolls internally instead of drifting with the page. Confirmed live: scrolling the full page height, the sidebar's own top edge stays fixed at the header's bottom edge throughout.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 45-test Playwright suite passing (30 homepage + 15 PLP viewports, all green). ### 5x · PLP: PAGE_SIZE raised to 9 so pagination only appears past a full page, sidebar and grid now lock together on scroll — 2026-08-29
Two more owner reports, same day as 5v/5w.

**Pagination threshold**: "My ask was to show 9 on the main page and then if there any more show it to the next page, by default our landing PLP would have 9 items, if 9 or less then 9 we willn not show the pagination." 5v's own `PAGE_SIZE = 3` technically had all 9 cards in the data and real pagination, but split them into 3 pages of 3 — from the landing page alone (page 1) it looked like only 3 products existed until a page number was clicked, which read as "you still did not add 9 products." Fixed: `PAGE_SIZE` raised to `9` (a full 3×3 grid on page one), and `ProductGrid` now only renders `<Pagination>` at all when `totalPages > 1` — with today's 9 cards that's a single page and no pagination row, exactly matching "if 9 or less then 9 we will not show the pagination." Pagination reappears automatically the moment a 10th card is added to a category's `styleCards`.

**Sidebar/grid scroll lock**: "the left panel change its po[s]ition and then gets fixed, it should be fixed at its original position. When scroll on the right side mean the product grid only scroll." Root cause: `categoryFilters.list` was the only sticky column in the filters+grid row (`sticky top-[111px] self-start`, 5k/5v/5w) — `ProductGrid`'s own root was a plain block. Both naturally scroll together with the page before the sidebar's sticky point engages, but the moment the sidebar locked at `top-111`, the grid (still a normal block) kept scrolling normally underneath it — the two visibly drifted apart right when the sidebar "caught up and stopped," which read as the sidebar itself doing something wrong. Fixed by giving `productGrid.root` the exact same `xl:sticky xl:top-[111px] xl:self-start xl:max-h-[calc(100vh-135px)] xl:overflow-y-auto` treatment as the sidebar — both columns now lock at the identical scroll position simultaneously, and past that point neither column's own box moves again; only the grid's real content (9 cards taller than the capped box, same as the sidebar's own tall expanded accordion) scrolls inside its own `overflow-y-auto`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 45-test Playwright suite passing. A scripted Playwright scroll check (`scrollTo` at several depths, then `page.mouse.wheel` over the grid) confirmed: sidebar and grid report the identical `getBoundingClientRect().top` at every scroll depth (621→421→221→111→71, matching exactly, no drift); once at the page's max scroll, wheeling over the grid moves only the grid's own internal `scrollTop` (0→684) while `window.scrollY` stays fixed at the page's own max; all 9 cards remain reachable via that internal scroll.

### 5af · Sitewide page-transition crossfade, reference cuberto.com/services — 2026-08-29
Owner request: apply the page-to-page transition cuberto.com/services uses, "observe it properly and apply it." Watched the reference live (real browser + source): a fixed, full-viewport white layer (`cb-loader-fill`, `background:#fff`, `z-index:999`) fades in over the outgoing page, the new page swaps in underneath while covered, then the layer fades back out to reveal it — a full-page crossfade, no directional slide in either direction; the nav stays static throughout.

Confirmed this Next.js install (16.3.2) ships React's native `<ViewTransition>` out of the box (present in `next/dist/compiled/react`'s bundled React; documented at `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/view-transitions.md`) — App Router route navigations are already React Transitions, so `<ViewTransition>` animates them with zero router-event wiring or custom overlay div. This is the framework-idiomatic approach, not a hand-rolled transition library, consistent with this project's existing "CSS-first motion" precedent (`Marquee`, `TextReveal`, `RevealBox`).

New `components/PageTransition.tsx` — a thin `<ViewTransition enter="page-fade-in" exit="page-fade-out" default="none">` wrapper. Per Next's own guide, the wrapper must live in each `page.tsx`, not `app/layout.tsx` (layouts persist across navigations, so their own enter/exit never fires) — wired into `app/page.tsx` and `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx` (the only 2 real content routes; `/styleguide` is a dev-only QA page, not part of real site navigation, left unwrapped). Wraps only `<main>`/`<Footer>`, not `<Header>` — matching the reference (its nav never moves) and Next's own "anchor the header" pattern: `Header.tsx`'s root now carries `style={{ viewTransitionName: "site-header" }}` unconditionally, tracked as a stable, non-animating anchor.

New CSS in `app/globals.css`: `::view-transition-old(.page-fade-out)` fades out fast (220ms), `::view-transition-new(.page-fade-in)` fades in slower (260ms) with a 220ms delay so it waits for the old content to finish leaving first — the gap between them shows only the page's own white `bg-paper`, reproducing the reference's "flash to white between pages" using the browser's own crossfade mechanics rather than a hand-built overlay. `::view-transition-group/-old/-new(site-header)` suppress the header's own animation entirely. `::view-transition { pointer-events: none; }` keeps the live page clickable immediately (per the guide). The sitewide reduced-motion block gained an explicit `::view-transition-old(*)/-new(*)/-group(*)` override — the existing `*` universal-selector rule doesn't reach the `::view-transition` pseudo-element tree (it renders in a separate top-layer, not the normal DOM).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live in the browser: `document.startViewTransition` supported; header computes `view-transition-name: site-header`; a real `<Link>` click (not a full reload) between `/` and `/activewear/leggings` fires `document.startViewTransition`, and `document.getAnimations()` shows an extra animation running for the expected ~300ms+ window (matching the 220+260ms budget) before settling on the destination page with the correct URL/title. Scoped Playwright overflow check clean at mobile/tablet/desktop across all 3 real routes (9/9).

This affected not just the divider but `FabricOptions`' entire content (heading block, table, note) — all of it was rendering `888px` wide, confirmed live before this fix (`section`'s own outer width measured `1164px`, i.e. the cap, with `138px` padding already eating into it).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live at 1440×900: divider width computes exactly `1164px`, `138px` insets both sides; `FabricOptions`' own section outer box computes `1440px` (its real content now correctly `1164px`). ### 5ay · PageTransition: slowed and smoothed the page-fade timing — 2026-08-29
Owner report: "the animation you built for changing one page to another is not accurate, the speed is very fast and jerky." Root cause was two things at once, not just speed: the original pairing (220ms `ease-in` exit, 260ms `ease-out` enter) used two different durations *and* two different acceleration curves back to back, which reads as two abrupt snaps rather than one continuous motion — "jerky" was describing the mismatched easing, not only the short duration.

`app/globals.css`'s `::view-transition-old(.page-fade-out)`/`::view-transition-new(.page-fade-in)` rules changed to `380ms`/`420ms`, both sharing one `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)` curve — the enter's delay still matches the exit's own duration exactly (`380ms`), so the sequence stays exit → brief hold at white → enter, the same reference-matching structure `PageTransition.tsx` was already built on, just no longer feeling rushed or mismatched.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Confirmed live via the CSS OM (`document.styleSheets`) after a real client-side navigation (`/` → `/activewear/leggings`): both rules present with the expected `0.38s`/`0.42s` durations and the shared `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)` easing on each. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 5az · Accordion: real bug, collapsed items had uneven top/bottom spacing — 2026-08-30
Owner report, reproduced on both the homepage and the Activewear PLP FAQ built the same day: "i checked on the homepage, it looks uneven there too, from the top it looks lesser than the bottom of the question. When the question is expanded then it looks fine. Recheck the component both collapsed and expanded state and fix it everywhere."

Root cause: `accordion.item`'s panel stays mounted at zero height when collapsed (`grid-rows-[0fr]`, needed for the open/close animation), but the 24px/16px spacing between the trigger and that panel lived as a flex `gap` on `item` itself — a flex `gap` reserves its full value even when the sibling is zero height, so a collapsed item measured `32px` above the question (padding-top only) but `57px` below it (padding-bottom + the gap the invisible panel still claimed).

Fixed by moving that spacing off `item`'s flex `gap` and onto `answer`'s own `mt-4`/`xl:mt-6` instead — it now lives inside `panelInner`'s existing `overflow-hidden` wrapper, so at collapsed (zero) height it's genuinely clipped to nothing rather than merely invisible, and renders exactly as before once expanded (same technique as the panel's own zero-height clipping, just applied one level lower). This is a shared component (`components/Accordion.tsx`/`components/ui/styles.ts`'s `accordion` recipe) — fixing it here fixes it everywhere it's used, no separate PLP-only patch.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport permanent Playwright suite passing. ### 5bc · PageTransition: second timing correction, this time backed by measurement — 2026-08-30
5ay's fix (380ms/420ms, shared easing) still wasn't right — owner: "still jerkey. Not at all the same as reference. What do you need from me to see or understand the reference better?" Asked the owner for a screen recording instead; they supplied `reference/Page animation.mov`.

Extracted 24 frames across the clip (`AVFoundation`/Swift, no `ffmpeg` on this machine — same technique already used for this project's other `.mov` references) and compared them directly rather than estimating. What they actually show: a brief white flash between pages (the same exit/hold/enter structure this component already had, so that part wasn't wrong), then the destination page's own H1 plays its own word-by-word reveal *on top of* the fade-in, which is why the whole sequence reads as slow and considered even though the base crossfade itself isn't multi-second. This project's own `TextReveal` (`components/TextReveal.tsx`) already does exactly that on every page's real H1 (`CategoryBanner`'s included, confirmed by checking its own source), so no new mechanism was needed there — only the base crossfade's own timing needed one more round of slowing: exit `450ms` (was `380ms`), enter `550ms` (was `420ms`), delayed `450ms` to match the exit exactly, same shared `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)` easing as 5ay's own fix (that part was already correct, not touched again).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Confirmed live via the CSS OM after a real client-side navigation: both rules present with the expected `0.45s`/`0.55s` durations and the shared easing curve. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.
