### 5bd · New bug from PageTransition: navigating to a shorter page landed on its footer, not its top — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "it even got wrong, when i go to leggings, it is stuck on the footer." Reproduced immediately and precisely: scrolled home to its own max scroll (~13294px), clicked through to `/activewear/leggings` (a shorter page, max scroll ~7083px), and the new page landed at `scrollY: 7083.5` — exactly that page's own max scroll, i.e. the browser had kept the *old* raw scroll offset across the navigation and simply clamped it to whatever the new, shorter document allowed, instead of resetting to the top the way a normal route change should.

Root cause: `PageTransition`'s `<ViewTransition>` wrapper (5's own page-fade work) means Next's built-in scroll-to-top-on-navigation logic (in its own `layout-router.js`) isn't reliably running. Confirmed live, step by step: called `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` manually after such a navigation and it had *zero effect*, even seconds later — not a timing race, the plain call simply wasn't landing. Switching to `window.scrollTo({top: 0, left: 0, behavior: "instant"})` (overriding this site's own global `scroll-behavior: smooth`) fixed it when called manually, which is what pointed at the real fix.

New `components/ScrollReset.tsx` (client component, rendered once in `app/layout.tsx` next to the existing `AppEntryMarker` — same "small, side-effect-only, mounted once in the root layout" shape): a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on `usePathname()` calls that same instant `scrollTo` on every pathname change — `useLayoutEffect`, not `useEffect`, because the deferred timing of a plain effect left the same window where the stale scroll offset gets clamped before the reset can run. Deliberately skips the very first render (initial page load shouldn't force a scroll) and skips browser back/forward navigations (tracked via a `popstate` listener) — those already get the browser's own correct native scroll restoration, which this must not fight.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-reproduced the exact failing scenario from the bug report (scroll home to max, click through to leggings) and confirmed `scrollY: 0` on arrival, where it previously read `7083.5`. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 5bh · Category page's Footer was bleeding through at the very top of the page — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "when i go to leggings page, it still shows th fixed footer." Reproduced immediately: loading `/activewear/leggings` showed the full site footer (logo, nav columns, "Get in touch," copyright) right underneath the breadcrumb, before any of the page's own real content (CategoryBanner's trust bullets, the product grid, etc.) — not a scroll-position issue at all, a real rendering bug, unrelated to 5bg's scroll fix.

Root cause: `Footer.tsx`'s own header comment spells out its "reveal" trick exactly — the footer is `sticky bottom-0 z-0`, which pins it to the viewport's bottom edge for the *entire* scroll range by itself; the only thing that makes it look like it's hidden until the real end of the page is `<main>` carrying `relative z-10` plus an opaque background, so every section's own background keeps covering the footer during normal scrolling. `app/page.tsx`'s own `<main>` already has `relative z-10 bg-paper ...` for exactly this reason. The category page template's `<main>` had neither class at all — so its footer had nothing covering it, and sat visible at the sticky position closest to the top of the (still mostly empty, still-rendering) page.

Fixed by adding `className="relative z-10 bg-paper"` to `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`'s `<main>`, matching the homepage's own `<main>` exactly (that page's own extra drop-shadow class wasn't copied — no Figma/owner reason for it here, this was purely about restoring the z-index/background relationship the footer's reveal trick actually depends on).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings`: fresh load now shows the real page content (banner, trust bullets, product grid) immediately below the breadcrumb, no footer content visible until scrolled there; scrolling to the real bottom (`scrollY: 7074` of `7974` total) confirmed the footer's own text ("Get in touch") is present and visible at that point, i.e. the reveal trick works correctly again. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

**CategoryBanner** ("fix the nav"): at mobile widths, `categoryBanner.section` was an unconditional fixed `h-[340px]` with `contentWrap` unconditionally `absolute`-positioned. Fixed structurally, not by shrinking any type size (no mobile Figma frame exists for this section, so inventing a smaller H1 would have been a real design guess, not a bug fix): below `xl`, `section` is `h-auto` with real padding and `breadcrumbWrap`/`contentWrap` are `static` (normal flow, box grows to fit real content); at `xl` the original fixed-height/absolute-centred desktop layout is untouched.

**FabricOptions** ("fix fabric options text alignment"): row 1's three cells are real `<th scope="col">` elements (from the SEO/AEO finalization pass, 5be) — the browser's own UA stylesheet centre-aligns `<th>` by default — while every row below uses plain `<td>`s, left-aligned by default. With no explicit alignment anywhere in the recipe, row 1 alone rendered centred against every other row's left alignment. Fixed with one `text-left` on `fabricOptions.headerRow`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings` at 375×812: CategoryBanner's content now grows with the box, clean break to the white section below, no overlap. At 1440×900: banner height still measures exactly `340px` (unchanged), and the fabric table's header row now computes `text-align: left`, matching the data rows. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30). `npm run build` clean.

### 5bk · The real "nav" bug: Header's non-sticky variant painting behind Footer's reveal trick — 2026-08-30
Owner report, after 5bj: "nav still is wront, please check", followed by a screenshot showing the header cut off (logo sliced in half, a stray white band above it) at the top of `/activewear/leggings`. 5bj's CategoryBanner fix was correct and re-verified (clean at every supported viewport, 360px–1920px) but did not address this — this was a completely different, genuinely new bug, only visible at certain viewport *heights* (e.g. 1280×720), which is why it hadn't shown up in the width-only Playwright suite or in the live Browser-pane tool's own screenshots (both of which happened to land on unaffected heights, plus that tool's own repeat flakiness this session made it look like another false alarm at first).

Root cause, found by comparing `elementFromPoint` at the very top of the viewport against `closest('header')`/`closest('footer')`: the point resolved inside `<footer>`, not `<header>`. `Footer`'s own `sticky bottom-0 z-0` reveal trick (see its own header comment, and 5bh above) computes as pinned near the viewport's top edge at scroll position 0 on *every* page, confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect` on both this page and the homepage — that part is by design, always covered by `<main>`'s own `relative z-10`. What's specific to this page: its `Header` renders with `sticky={false}` (the "gymshark trial", 2026-08-29), which uses `header.baseStatic` — plain `position: static`, no z-index at all. A `static` element paints in an *earlier* CSS stacking bucket than any positioned one, regardless of z-index value, so this header was painting BEHIND Footer's positioned (`z-0`) box wherever they overlapped — the top ~87px of the page. The homepage's own header (`sticky z-40`) never showed this, since a positioned element with a positive z-index always paints after Footer's z-0 one; only this page's plain-`static` variant was exposed.

Fixed with one class change: `header.baseStatic` gained `relative z-40` (`components/ui/styles.ts`) — `relative` (not `sticky`) keeps this variant's own non-sticky, scroll-away layout completely unchanged; it only opts the element into normal stacking-context ordering so its own `z-40` (matching the sticky header's own value) can do its job. Confirmed via a fresh Playwright check (not the live Browser-pane tool, given its repeat flakiness this session) that `elementFromPoint` at the top of the viewport now resolves inside `<header>`, at both 1280×720 (the exact reported viewport) and the previously-working 375×812/1440×900, and that Footer's own reveal-at-the-real-bottom mechanic (5bh) still works correctly on both.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30), including 1280×720. `npm run build` clean.

### 5bq · TextReveal: real bug, an embedded "\n" scrambled word order instead of breaking the line — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "fabric section title should be in 2 lines." It was rendering 3 lines, and once past that, the words after the break rendered out of order ("The fabrics behind the brands" / "big" instead of "The fabrics behind the" / "big brands").

Two layered causes, both fixed:
- **Container too narrow**: `FabricOptions`' `headingBlock` was `max-w-[579px]` at every breakpoint (matching `WhatWeCover`'s own centred column, copied without re-checking against this heading's own longer first line). At 579px, even "The fabrics behind the" alone wrapped a second time. Widened to `xl:max-w-[750px]` (measured live: the minimum width that first line fits on one line at this heading's 54px desktop size; 700px still wrapped, 750px didn't). Mobile kept its own confirmed 579px, unaffected.
- **The real bug**: `FabricOptions` renders its heading through `TextReveal` (the word-by-word entrance animation), and `TextReveal.tsx` split words with a bare `text.split(" ")` — a `"\n"` embedded in `Category.fabricHeading` doesn't contain a space, so `"the\nbig"` survived as one single "word" token, embedded whole inside that word's own `overflow-hidden` animated mask span. That's not merely "failed to break" — an embedded newline inside a masked, animated inline-block scrambled the actual rendered line order, which is why raising the container width alone wasn't enough. Fixed in `TextReveal.tsx` itself: split on `"\n"` first into lines, render a real `<br />` between them, then split each line into words as before (reveal-index keeps incrementing globally across the break, so the stagger animation is unaffected). This is a shared, sitewide component — the fix works for any future heading that needs a forced line break through `TextReveal`, not just this one string. `fabricOptions.heading` lost the `whitespace-pre-line`/`text-wrap` overrides tried first (the real fix made them unnecessary — the break is a real `<br />` now, not a literal text character needing special CSS handling).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport permanent Playwright suite passing (`TextReveal` is used sitewide — Hero and every other reveal heading, none of which use embedded newlines, are confirmed unaffected). Live-checked: rendered DOM now shows a real `<br>` between "the" and "big" with each word in its own clean, unmodified mask; heading height computes to exactly 2 lines at both mobile (`68px` / `34px` line-height) and desktop (`128px` / `64px` line-height).

### 5bv · Section divider: stale 120px margin corrected to 72px, no double-stacking with WhatWeCover's own spacing — 2026-08-30
Owner: "what we cover should have 72px gap from the top of heading to the top section, there is extra space remove everything unecessary." Live-measured before this fix: mobile had `232px` from the divider to the heading, desktop `174px` — both bloated by the same root cause.

The page-level divider's own `mb-[120px]` was a flat, unconditional value written back when `WhatWeCover` was the LAST of the 3 sections (pre-5as reorder), stacking with whatever came after it. Once `WhatWeCover` moved to be FIRST (5as), its own `section` recipe grew a real, Figma-confirmed `py-[72px]` on mobile specifically so it could supply its own top spacing — but the divider's stale `mb-[120px]` kept firing too, doubling mobile's gap (`120` divider + `72` own padding = `192` before the eyebrow/heading gap on top of that). Desktop's `WhatWeCover` has no top padding of its own by design (relies entirely on this divider), so its own `120px` was simply the wrong number post-reorder, never re-checked against the new `72px` target.

Fixed: divider margin is now `xl:mb-[72px]` with none below `xl` — mobile relies purely on `WhatWeCover`'s own confirmed `72px` section padding (no stacking), desktop gets the requested `72px` divider gap directly since that's its only spacing source.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport permanent Playwright suite passing. ### 5bw · FabricOptions mobile accordion: real transition-snap + title-shift bug fixed — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "The transition of opening and closing the container should be very smooth. Also, when I expand the content, the title moves a little, nothing should move from its position." Both were real bugs in `5bp`'s first build, not tuning:

1. **No animation at all, just a snap.** The first build branched to two entirely different markup shapes per row — an open `<div>` with its detail block mounted straight at `grid-rows-[1fr]`, or a closed `<button>` with no detail block in the DOM whatsoever. A CSS transition can't animate a value into existence on first mount, and can't animate a value out on unmount either — so opening/closing just snapped. Fixed by mounting the SAME structure (header + detail block) for every row always, exactly like `CategoryFilters`' own groups, toggling only the grid track class (`accordionDetailGrid` 0fr ↔ `accordionDetailGridOpen` 1fr) — now there's a real starting frame for the transition to animate from/to.
2. **Title shifting 1px on toggle.** The closed row had `border border-[#e8ecf1]` (1px) and the open row had no border property at all — under `border-box` sizing, adding/removing a border shifts the interior content box by the border's own width, nudging the title. Fixed by keeping a `border` (1px) present at BOTH states on a new shared `accordionItem` wrapper class, only ever toggling its *colour* (`accordionItemOpen`'s `border-transparent` vs `accordionItemClosed`'s `border-[#e8ecf1]`) — identical box geometry either way, so the title never moves. Also added `transition-colors duration-300` to that same wrapper so the bg/border swap itself animates too, not just the height, matching "very smooth."

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings` at 375px: title's `getBoundingClientRect()` top/left measured identical before and after collapsing the open row (no shift). `document.querySelectorAll('...').getAnimations()` on the toggled wrapper confirmed a real `CSSTransition` running with the correct keyframes (`border-color`/`background-color` interpolating to their real target values) — could not observe the animation's live frame-by-frame progress in this session's automated browser tab, since it reports `document.visibilityState: "hidden"` and Chrome freezes the compositor clock (rAF, CSS transitions) for backgrounded tabs; the keyframe data itself is the correctness proof, and the underlying technique is identical to `CategoryFilters`' own already-shipped, working accordion. Full 30-viewport `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` suite re-run against the fixed build.

On mobile, `FinalCta`'s ticker block (the compliance ticker, titled "STANDARD ON EVERY ORDER") is the first thing in that section — on the Activewear PLP it sits directly below `Faq`. `Faq`'s own `mobileSection` already carries the sitewide standard `pb-[72px]` ([02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md)'s documented mobile section-to-section rhythm); `finalCta.mobileTickerBlock` also carried its own `pt-[72px]`, stacking to a real 144px gap.

### 5bz · Real pagination bug: fixed "Previous" width let it overflow into the next cell — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "previous box is touching the other box, no spacing in between." First checked whether the row's own `gap-2` was actually applying — confirmed live at both 375px and the owner's own requested 360px width that it was, with a zoomed screenshot showing a clean 8px gap in both cases, so reported back that it couldn't be reproduced and asked for a screenshot. The owner's screenshot showed the real thing: "Previous"'s own text visibly ran edge-to-edge inside its box with no right-side padding, touching the orange "1" cell directly.

Root cause: `pagination.edgeCell` had `max-xl:w-[80px]`, a hard fixed width — but flex items default to `min-width: auto`, meaning a flex item never shrinks below its own content's minimum intrinsic size even when an explicit smaller `width` is set. The icon+gap+"Previous" content, once its own `px-5` padding is counted, genuinely needed more room than 80px could give it (confirmed live: real content measured `~107px`). Whenever font metrics pushed the content past that threshold (browser/device-dependent — this is exactly why it didn't reproduce in every test render), the button rendered wider than its own declared 80px, overflowing straight into the gap meant for the next cell.

Fixed by dropping the fixed width entirely: `max-xl:w-fit` (plus `whitespace-nowrap`) sizes the box to its real content instead, so it can never overflow itself regardless of font rendering. Desktop was never affected (no width class of its own at `xl`).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Fresh Playwright checks at 360px: "Previous" now measures its real content width (`~107px`, not the old fixed `80px`), with the row's own `8px` gap to "1" confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect` and a zoomed screenshot. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30). `npm run build` clean.

### 5ci · PDP: new `ProductInfo` text block (SKU + H1 + description), figma nodes 634:4988/638:2541 — 2026-08-31

### 5c PDP made a real, reusable, data-driven template — 2026-09-01
Owner request: build the PDP as a reusable template, not a one-off page — one route, one data shape, adding a new PDP later means adding one content entry, nothing else. Route/breadcrumb/`ProductInfo`/`ProductGallery` already existed from a concurrent session (this same day); this pass built on top of that rather than restarting (confirmed with the owner first), adding everything the owner's spec still required.

**Route**: `app/activewear/[category]/[product]/page.tsx` → `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx` (owner's own exact requested path) — pure rename, `product`/`style` swapped throughout `generateStaticParams`/`getData`/`params`, no behavior change. `rm -rf .next && npm run build` needed to clear Next's stale generated route types after the rename (the old `[product]` types otherwise linger and fail `tsc`).

**Content shape** (`StyleCard`, `content/activewear/types.ts`): four new optional fields, same "optional, graceful fallback, a card without one just doesn't render that piece" pattern the type already established for `pdpTitle`/`sku`/`pdpHeading`/`pdpDescription`/`images` — `pdpMetaTitle`, `pdpMetaDescription`, `material` (a short spec line for `productSchema()`, kept independent of the not-yet-built visual spec-datasheet section rather than a fragile guess at which future row means "material"), `faqs` (this style's own 2 to 3 questions). `images` itself gained an optional `src` alongside its existing `alt`, so a gallery slot can exist before its real photo file does.

**Shared PDP content** (`content/activewear/pdpShared.ts`, new file): `pdpFaqEntity` (the entity question, first on every PDP) and `pdpFaqOperational` (MOQ/grading, sample time, shipping, NDA, get-started, last on every PDP) — written once, reused unchanged, same pattern `content/site.ts` already sets for sitewide facts. Merge order on the page: `[pdpFaqEntity, ...styleCard.faqs, ...pdpFaqOperational]`, feeding both the visible `Faq` render and `faqSchema()` from the one array.

### 5cj · Google display-limit rules adopted sitewide; Leggings' double-pipe title fixed — 2026-08-30
Owner supplied a standing rule set for every page's title/description/slug/breadcrumb, keyed to Google's real pixel-width display limits (not hard data limits): title ~50-60 chars, form `"Custom [Style/Category] Manufacturer | Capriowear"`; description front-loaded in the first ~155 chars, may run to ~270 for AEO; H1 unshortened; slugs short/clean/permanent; breadcrumb labels short. Documented as a new `docs/06-seo.md` section (`2a · Google display limits`), referenced from the per-page checklist at the bottom of that file.

Audited both existing pages against it while writing the rule down:
- **Leggings PLP — real violation, fixed**: `metaTitle` was `"Custom Leggings Manufacturer | Private Label and OEM"`, rendering as a THREE-segment, 78-char title once the layout's own `" | Capriowear"` suffix appended (`"...Manufacturer | Private Label and OEM | Capriowear"`) — well past the ~65-char ceiling, and not the single-pipe form the new rule requires. Shortened to `"Custom Leggings Manufacturer"` (rendered: `"Custom Leggings Manufacturer | Capriowear"`, 42 chars) — "Private Label and OEM" already lives in `metaDescription`, well inside its own first-155-char window, so nothing essential was lost. `Category.metaTitle`'s own doc comment in `types.ts` updated to state the length rule directly, so a future category file doesn't repeat this.
- **Homepage — compliant, no change**: title (`DEFAULT_TITLE`, `content/site.ts`) is 66 chars total, but the keyword portion alone (`"Custom Activewear & Teamwear Manufacturer in Pakistan"`) is only 53 chars — a 60-char truncation only ever cuts into the `" | Capriowear"` suffix, never the keyword, which the rule explicitly allows. Description is 158 chars; the first 155 already carry every essential (manufacturer, OEM/ODM/private label, MOQ, sample lead time), truncating only mid-word on "worldwide" at the very tail. Left both as owner-supplied exact copy.
- **Leggings description**: 235 chars (within the ~270 AEO allowance); first 155 chars already front-load manufacturer + service model + MOQ + fabric spec. Not changed.
- **Slug (`leggings`) and breadcrumb (`Home > Activewear > Leggings`)**: already short and clean, no change needed.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live `document.title`/`meta[name=description]` read on both `/` and `/activewear/leggings`:
- Homepage: title 66 chars (`"Custom Activewear & Teamwear Manufacturer in Pakistan | Capriowear"`), description 158 chars.
- Leggings: title now 41 chars (`"Custom Leggings Manufacturer | Capriowear"`), description 235 chars (unchanged).

`productGallery.desktopRoot` gained `xl:sticky xl:top-[111px] xl:self-start`. `top-[111px]` reuses this project's own already-established offset for a sticky column beside a sitewide sticky header (87px header + 24px gap) — the same value `categoryFilters.list` used before its own non-sticky-header trial (this PDP's header is sticky by default, unlike the PLP's). `self-start` stops the column stretching to match `ProductInfo`'s own height, which would break sticky the same way `categoryFilters.list`'s own comment already documents. Mobile untouched (`hidden` below `xl`, gallery isn't even rendered there — mobile has its own separate markup).

Fresh Playwright check on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 1440×900: gallery computes `position: sticky`, `top: 111px`, and its own `getBoundingClientRect().top` stays pinned at `111` through a 300px scroll; confirmed via screenshot at a 400px scroll that the header has scrolled off while the gallery image stays pinned near the top and the info panel (Fabric options, Customization, Related styles) scrolls up beside it — matching the reference. Releases naturally near the bottom of its own flex row once the row's remaining content is shorter than the gallery itself, same standard sticky-column behavior the reference site's own page shows, not a bug. ### 5ce · PDP sticky gallery: real bug, growing gap as the header hid on scroll — 2026-09-02
Owner report, same day as 5cd: "gap on top of the image, it should be 24px gap max and then stays sticky, currently it get fixed on almost 24px and when scroll more, its adds more gap."

Root cause: 5cd's own `top-[111px]` offset (87px header + 24px gap) assumed the sitewide `Header` stays visible and 87px tall for the whole scroll, the same assumption `CategoryFilters`' own sidebar made before its own header stopped being reliably present. But this PDP's `Header` doesn't just stay sticky — it also hides itself on scroll-down past its own height (`Header.tsx`'s own translate-away behavior, unrelated to this task). Once it hides, the 87px it used to occupy is gone, but the gallery's own `top` offset stayed fixed at 111px regardless — the empty space where the header used to be read as a gap that kept growing the further down the page the owner scrolled, exactly as reported.

Fixed by dropping the offset to a flat `xl:top-[24px]` — correct whether the header is currently visible or hidden, since sticky only actually engages well after the header has already crossed its own hide threshold on a normal downward scroll (no overlap in practice).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean. Fresh Playwright check on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 1440×900: gallery's own `getBoundingClientRect().top` stays exactly `24` through both a 200px and a 500px scroll (previously would have read `111` then grown as the header hid), and releases naturally once past its own row's height at deeper scroll depths (900px/1400px), same expected sticky-column behavior as before — confirmed via screenshot that the header is fully scrolled away with no leftover empty space above the gallery. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30).

### 5cm · ProductCtas mobile bar: real overlap bug with Footer's social icons, fixed by switching fixed → sticky — 2026-09-02
Owner report: "the fixed cta, overlapping the social icons on the footer. It should not do that." Root cause: the mobile bar was `position: fixed` with `z-30`, explicitly documented as outranking both `<main>`'s own `z-10` and `Footer`'s own `sticky bottom-0 z-0` reveal trick — but being a *descendant* of `<main>`, and `<main>`'s own stacking already always outranking Footer regardless of the bar's internal z-index, the bar kept floating on top of Footer forever, including over the social icons.

Fixed by splitting `ProductCtas` into two components: `ProductCtas` (desktop row, unchanged, stays in the text column) and a new `ProductCtasMobileBar` (mobile bar, `position: sticky bottom-0`, rendered as the literal last child of `<main>` instead of alongside the desktop row). Sticky + correct DOM position mirrors `Footer`'s own reveal trick in reverse: sticks to the viewport bottom throughout the page's own scroll range (same "always visible" behavior as before), releases the instant `<main>` ends — exactly at the Footer boundary. The old `bg-ink` spacer div (reserving space for the out-of-flow fixed bar) is gone — sticky occupies its own real space in flow, so the bar's own box does that job now.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 375px: bar confirmed still pinned to viewport bottom at scrollY 2000; at full-page scroll, bar box and the footer's social icon boxes confirmed non-overlapping (bar ends ~y566, icons start ~y727), also confirmed visually via screenshot.

### 5cn · ProductCtasMobileBar slides away automatically at the CTA section — 2026-09-02
Owner follow-up, same day as 5cm: "when it gets to the cta section, remove the fixed cta automatically." `ProductCtasMobileBar` became a client component: an invisible marker (`FINAL_CTA_MARKER_ID`) renders immediately before `<FinalCta>`; the bar watches it via `IntersectionObserver` and slides out (`translate-y-full`) the instant it enters the viewport — earlier than the sticky/DOM-position release from 5cm (which only happens at the very end of `<main>`, after FinalCta). `translate-y-full` rather than `hidden` keeps the bar's own 60px reserving its space in flow, so nothing below it jumps.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 375px: bar confirmed still pinned (`translate: none`) at scrollY 2000; once scrolled so the FinalCta marker is near the viewport, `getComputedStyle(bar).translate` reads `0px 100%` and the bar's own bounding box sits fully below the viewport (y = 812, the viewport height) — confirmed visually via screenshot, FinalCta's own CTA button visible with no floating duplicate bar. (Note: Tailwind v4's `translate-y-*` utilities set the CSS `translate` property, not `transform` — verify against `getComputedStyle(el).translate`.)

### 5co · ProductCtasMobileBar's auto-hide condition fixed (was reappearing under the FAQ CTA); now shared by PLP and PDP — 2026-09-02
Owner report: "it disappears but when you scroll under the faq cta, its still sticky on the bottom. Also it should be disappear when I am close or coming to the let's build custom collection text." Two real bugs in 5cn's own hide condition, both found live: (1) `entry.isIntersecting` alone reverted to `false` (re-showing the bar) once the marker scrolled back out of the viewport above — backwards, it should stay hidden for the rest of the page. (2) A same-day first fix comparing `entry.boundingClientRect.top < window.innerHeight` directly is wrong in practice: `IntersectionObserver` only fires at threshold crossings, so that raw `top` value is stale between crossings — read at the "approaching" crossing it was still `~927` against an 812px viewport, evaluating false, and didn't get another chance to correct itself until the next crossing far later, leaving the bar visible the entire time in between (exactly the reported bug). Fixed by keying off `entry.isIntersecting || entry.boundingClientRect.top < 0` instead — true from the moment the section is approached and never flips back while continuing to scroll down.

Also, owner: "it should be applied to both plp and pdp" — the PLP (`app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`) previously hand-duplicated the sticky-bar markup from the `productCtas` recipe directly rather than importing `ProductCtasMobileBar`, so it had none of the FinalCta-approach auto-hide behavior at all. It now renders the real `ProductCtasMobileBar` component plus its own `FINAL_CTA_MARKER_ID` marker before its own `FinalCta`, identical to the PDP.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean. Live on both `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` and `/activewear/leggings` at 375px, sampled at 6 scroll checkpoints (far before the marker, approaching, at the marker, past it while still in FinalCta, near the footer, and scrolled back up above the marker): bar translate reads `none` (visible) only before the marker and after scrolling back above it, `0px 100%` (hidden) at every position at or beyond approaching the marker — confirmed monotonic, no reappearing. Full 30-viewport `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` suite passing.

### 5cp · ProductCtasMobileBar: collapsed state now resets on scroll-up — 2026-09-02
Owner report: "it works now but it does not come back when I scroll back up." Root cause: the `collapsed` state (which zeroes the bar's own height ~300ms after it hides, so no stray gap is left between FinalCta and Footer) was only ever set to `true`, never reset back to `false` when `nearFinalCta` reverted on scroll-up — so once collapsed, the bar had no real box left to slide back into, permanently.

Fixed using React's own "adjusting state during render" pattern (a `prevNearFinalCta` comparison) instead of calling `setCollapsed` synchronously inside an effect body, which `eslint`'s `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule correctly flags as a cascading-render risk. The reset now happens immediately, in the same render `nearFinalCta` goes false; the 300ms delayed collapse (the other direction) stays in its own effect, unchanged.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 375px: scrolled past the marker, bar confirmed `translate: 0px 100%`, `height: 0` (collapsed); scrolled back up above the marker, bar confirmed `translate: none`, `height: 60` — fully restored.

### 5cw · Pagination hover/scroll fixes; tablet-width (768–1279px) fixes for PLP card images, mobile CTA bar, and mobile-only artwork — 2026-09-03
Owner: pagination page-number hover should match the "Download Catalog" CTA's own hover color, and clicking a page number should land on the product-list top, not stay mid-page. Separately: several components tuned only for real mobile width read badly once the same `max-xl:`/unprefixed styles carried through to real tablet width (768–1279px) too — PLP card images stretched, the sticky mobile CTA bar stretched full-bleed, and a few sections' mobile-only placeholder images grew disproportionately tall.

**Pagination** (`pagination.cell`/`edgeCell`, `components/ui/styles.ts`) gained `hover:bg-[#FFF6F3]` (`edgeCell` as `enabled:hover:`, so the disabled edge button shows no hover), matching `productOptions.secondaryDesktop`'s own confirmed hover color.

**Scroll-to-top, three attempts, the third one right**: (1) `gridRef.current.scrollIntoView()` called synchronously in the same tick as `setPage` measured the grid's stale, pre-render layout — confirmed live to land back at the pagination row. (2) Deferred one `requestAnimationFrame` and used `behavior: "smooth"` — still confirmed live, twice (both directions), to land wrong. (3) The real bug was never the timing or the animation mode — it was the *target*. The owner's own words, "land me ... on top of the title where it says sports bras," name `CategoryMetaStrip`'s own title block, a sibling rendered by the page *above* `ProductGrid`, not the grid itself. `CategoryMetaStrip`'s root gained `id="plp-title"`; `ProductGrid.tsx`'s `handlePageChange` now calls `document.getElementById("plp-title").scrollIntoView({behavior: "smooth", block: "start"})` inside a double `requestAnimationFrame` (measure only after the browser has actually painted the new page). `#plp-title`'s own position never moves with page/card count, so both directions land correctly and animate smoothly — owner confirmed, then explicitly asked for the animation back once the target was fixed, since a stable target has nothing left for `behavior: "smooth"` to race against.

**Tablet-width fixes** (`components/ui/styles.ts`), each the same shape — a value tuned for real phone width was scoped `max-xl:`/unprefixed, so it also applied through 1279px tablet width where the same box is much wider:
- `productCard.image`'s fixed `220px` height: `max-xl:` → `max-md:`. From `md:` (768px) up, falls through to its own `aspect-[79/100]` ratio (same as desktop) instead of staying pinned to 220px and reading squashed.
- `productCtas.mobileButton`'s `w-full`: kept below `md:`, capped `md:w-auto md:min-w-[280px] md:mx-auto` from `md:` up — the bar itself stays full-bleed/sticky at any width, only the button inside stops stretching.
- `trustPoints.artworkWrap`, `whatWeCover.artworkWrap` (both `xl:hidden`, no desktop frame exists for either), and `whatWeMake.mobileTile`: each gained `md:max-w-[420px] md:mx-auto` — the fixed `16:11` ratio was correct at real phone width, but the same `w-full` box stretched to as much as ~880px tall once the container reached 1279px tablet width otherwise.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live at 1024px: PLP card image measures `79:100` (was pinned `220px` tall); CTA bar button measures `280px`, centered (was full-width); `WhatWeCover`/`TrustPoints` artwork both measure `420×261px`, proportional (was stretching to the container's own width). Confirmed unaffected at 375px (real mobile, all three unchanged) and 1440px (`xl:` desktop, all three unchanged). Full `npx playwright test tests/screenshots.spec.ts` (30 tests, home + styleguide, including that suite's own 768/810/820/834px tablet viewports) passes clean — this task's actual PLP/PDP changes aren't in that suite (per its own established scope, see Phase 3 below), so this only confirms no regression at the suite's covered pages/widths.

### 5dg · Inside the Factory: a real tablet-only gallery layout, not the desktop carousel reused verbatim — 2026-09-03
Owner, continuing the homepage tablet-width review, three messages in sequence: (1) "Inside the factory should also use desktop version"; (2) after that broke centering at 768px: implicit correction via live testing catching the bug myself before the owner had to; (3) "thinside the factory, image placeholder should have same gap from the left as other sections have as default state, image sise should be resonable, currently too wide, in the default state, 2nd image peak should be visible so customer knows its scrollable."

First pass moved `insideFactory.desktopOuter`/`mobileSection` from `xl:` to `md:`, matching the split already used for Hero's ticker/Certified & Compliant's desktop row. This alone broke centering: the desktop card's `calc(50% - 475px)` padding goes negative below a 950px viewport and is silently clamped to 0px, so the first card rendered flush left with no peek at 768px (caught live before shipping). A second attempt shrank the card to 600px (half 300, keeping the calc positive) but kept the centered-snap technique — wrong per the owner's own follow-up: centering doesn't match the rest of the page's left-aligned sections, and doesn't reliably peek a second card at rest.

Final: a genuinely different tablet layout, not a scaled desktop one. `TABLET_CARD_WIDTH`/`TABLET_CARD_GAP` (520×24, `InsideFactory.tsx`) replace the desktop 950×48 at `md:`; `insideFactory.desktopRow` uses a flat `px-8` (32px, matching `container-p`'s own `md:` inset) instead of the centering calc, `desktopCard` uses `snap-start` instead of `snap-center`. `xl:` (1280px+) is completely unchanged — same 950×550 card, same centered calc, same `snap-center`. The chevron's click-to-scroll `cardPitch` is now a `matchMedia("(min-width: 1280px)")`-driven state (same pattern `ProductGrid.tsx` already uses for its own page size) instead of a fixed constant, so a chevron click scrolls the right distance at either tier.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live, at rest (`scrollLeft: 0`): 32px left inset at both 768px and 1024px (was 84px/212px centering padding, then 0px before that); a real second-card peek at both widths (card 2 starts at x=576 of 768px; fully visible by 1024px with card 3 peeking at x=1120). `xl:` desktop (1440px) unchanged: 245px centering padding, 950×550 card. Real mobile (<768px) still renders its own separate swipeable carousel, unaffected. Full `npx playwright test tests/screenshots.spec.ts` (30 tests) passes.

### 5dj · Inside the Factory tablet image ratio matched to Exhibitions'; a real xl: regression caught before shipping — 2026-09-04
Owner, comparing the two sections again after 5di: "Inside the facoty image container size and behavior still look different than the exhibition contianer, make the foctory the same as exhibition."

Width/gap/left-inset already matched from 5di, but the image height still didn't: Inside the Factory's own real `19:11` ratio gives 271px tall at a 469px-wide card, Exhibitions' `469:320` gives 320px — same width, visibly different box. New `insideFactory.desktopCardMedia` token (`md:aspect-[469/320] xl:aspect-[19/11]`, passed as `className` on the tablet-tier `MediaPlaceholder`) overrides the ratio only at `md:`, matching Exhibitions exactly, while explicitly restoring this section's own real ratio at `xl:`.

**A real bug caught live before shipping**: the first version omitted the `xl:` half of that pair, on the wrong assumption that `md:` only applies within the 768-1279px range — `md:` is a min-width breakpoint, so it stays active at every wider screen too unless a later breakpoint overrides it. The real desktop gallery briefly rendered its 950px-wide card at 648px tall (matching Exhibitions' ratio) instead of the correct 550px (this section's own `19:11`) until `xl:aspect-[19/11]` was added back explicitly.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live: both sections now measure pixel-identical 469×320px at 768px and 1024px (card 2 at x=525 for both, unchanged left inset). `xl:` desktop (1440px) confirmed correct: 950×550, ratio 1.727 (`19:11`) — the regression was caught and fixed before this response shipped, not left in. ### 5dq · Homepage typography: desktop sizes extended to tablet width (768–1279px) — 2026-09-04
Owner, stepping back from the section-by-section spacing/layout fixes: "should we use the font sizes used on the desktop, currently i thik we are using the mobile ones, right?" — confirmed yes, every homepage heading/eyebrow/body pair follows a `max-xl:MOBILE xl:DESKTOP` shape, so tablet renders at mobile size the whole way to 1280px. Delegated the specific values: "i can leave it up to your judgment to increase the font sizes as per best practices for tablet, does that work?"

Plan-mode pass (see the approved plan for the full investigation): verified via `app/globals.css`'s own `clamp()` formulas that every fluid heading token (`--text-h1`, used by the shared `sectionHeading.heading`) already evaluates well above its paired mobile value at 768px — `text-h1` ≈ 39px vs. the 30px mobile value it replaces, growing continuously to 54px at 1440px — and every fixed-literal pair has desktop ≥ mobile by construction. So this was a mechanical, zero-new-values fix across the board: swap `max-xl:`/`xl:` for `max-md:`/`md:` on every pair found. ### 5dx · Faq heading: 3-line wrap fixed for real, across every viewport (not just the ones spot-checked) — 2026-09-06
Follow-up to 5dw: owner reported "still in 3 lines," then "it should be fixed on all viewports." 5dw only checked 375px and 1440px; the actual bug was in `desktopHeading`'s width cap, unrelated to the alignment fix — `text-h1`'s font-size is a *continuous* fluid clamp (not stepped per breakpoint), so a single fixed-px `max-w` per breakpoint can only ever be correct at one point in each breakpoint's range. Two fixed-px attempts each broke somewhere else: `md:max-w-[320px]` (original) wrapped to 3 lines anywhere in `md:` (768–1279px) — confirmed live at both 768px and 1024px; raising it to `md:max-w-[360px]` fixed 768px but still wrapped to 3 lines at 1024px and 1279px, since the font is already ~45–50px there and needs ~440px, not 360px.

Replaced the three fixed-px caps (`md:max-w-[320/360px]`, `xl:max-w-[474px]`, `min-[1920px]:max-w-[500px]`) with one `em`-based value, `max-w-[8.85em]`. Live-measured the minimum width needed for "Top questions from" (the forced first line before `<br/>`) to hold one line at four widths — 342px/39.07px at 768, 440px/50.42px at 1279, 472px/54px at 1440, 564px/64.67px at 1920 — and found the ratio near-constant at ~8.73–8.75× the heading's own font-size (expected: same string, width scales with font-size). `em` is relative to the element's own font-size, so `8.85em` (a small buffer above the measured ratio) tracks `text-h1`'s clamp automatically at any width, with no breakpoint list to maintain or re-break.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` all clean. Live-measured line count at every breakpoint boundary and midpoint on the homepage: 349/375/600px (mobile heading, unaffected, still 2 lines) and 768/1024/1279/1440/1920px (desktop heading) all render exactly 2 lines, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect().height` against the computed `line-height` at each width — not just the two points 5dw had checked.

### 5dy · Nav bar desktop logo corrected to Figma's real stacked geometry — 2026-09-06
Owner shared a Figma link (node `680:392`, "Navigation") with: "update the caprio logo in the nav bar, not in the footer. Only for desktop." `get_design_context`/`get_metadata` on that node showed the real logo (node `680:394`) is `141x38`, with "WEAR" set BELOW "CAPRIO" (a 6px gap, offset `transform="translate(80 29)"`) — not beside it. The site's existing `Logo.tsx` (built 2026-09-02) turned out to be a mis-transcription: a flatter `217.2x22.76` combined mark with WEAR beside CAPRIO, decoded from a screenshot rather than a direct Figma export, rendered at only `h-5` (20px tall) in the header.

`Logo.tsx` gained a `stacked?: boolean` prop: unset keeps the exact original (approximated) geometry — still used by Footer, `MobileNav`'s drawer, `IntroLoader`, and the header's own mobile mark, none of which were in scope; `stacked` renders the real Figma paths at their own `141x38` combined viewBox. `Header.tsx` gained a new `desktopLogo` prop, rendered as a sibling of `logo` inside the same brand `<Link>` — each self-hides via its own recipe class (`header.brandLogo` gained `xl:hidden`; new `header.brandLogoDesktop` is `hidden xl:block h-[38px] w-auto`), the same "two parallel self-hiding elements" pattern already used elsewhere (e.g. `Faq`'s `desktopOuter`/`mobileSection`) rather than a JS conditional. All 8 pages rendering the real `Header` were updated to pass `desktopLogo={<Logo stacked className={header.brandLogoDesktop} />}` alongside the existing `logo` prop (styleguide's exploratory `HeaderOverlayNav` demo, a different component, was left untouched).

`h-[38px]` is the mark's real native Figma height, not a fitted-to-avoid-overflow value like `brandLogo`'s own `h-5` — at 38px tall it renders 141px wide (its true aspect ratio), actually narrower than `brandLogo`'s own ~190px-wide render at 20px tall, and shorter than the desktop nav's own 48px-tall trigger pills, so header row height and overflow risk are both unaffected.

**A pre-existing, unrelated overflow found and flagged, not fixed here**: `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` overflows horizontally at exactly 1280px viewport width (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth` 1360px against a 1280px viewport) — confirmed via a controlled test (temporarily reverting `brandLogoDesktop` back to `h-5`) that this persists with the logo change fully backed out, so it predates this task and is unrelated to it. Flagged as a separate background task rather than fixed in this session, since it's out of this request's scope (likely the PDP's own fixed `xl:w-[514px]` text column, per `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`'s own comment).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` all clean. Live: desktop (`xl:`, 1280–1920px) measures the real `141x38` viewBox at `x:80` (matching `container-p`'s inset); mobile/drawer (`<xl:`) unchanged, still the original `217.2x22.76` viewBox at 20px tall; Footer's own logo (both breakpoints) unchanged; homepage has zero overflow at 1280px (header itself measures exactly 1280px wide).

### 5ed · Eleventh and final Teamwear category, Rash Guards & Fight Wear, verified and wired live — 2026-09-06
Owner spec: build `/teamwear/fight-wear` as the last Teamwear category, same engine as every prior one (`app/teamwear/[sport]/page.tsx`/`[style]/page.tsx`, `categoryEntityFaq()`, `buildCtaSubline()`, the `structuredBlock` "decoration" variant) — no new routes or components. `content/teamwear/fight-wear.ts` was found already fully written (committed in `66e08e1`, the original catalog build) matching almost the entire brief verbatim, and `content/teamwear/sports.ts` already had it registered (`"fight-wear": fightWear`) — this task was a verification pass, not a build from scratch; no code diff resulted.

Confirmed against the brief: no en/em dashes, no "elastane"/"Lycra"/"seamless", no GSM figures asserted as hard numbers, no prices, Muay Thai/board shorts excluded as a card (fabric-table "on request" mention only), gym+athlete naming (never squad numbers), MOQ 50/style. All 5 style cards `"draft"` (cut-and-sew only: long-sleeve rash guard, MMA fight shorts, short-sleeve rash guard, grappling spats, compression top — closures/grip bands correctly excluded as sourced trims, not cards). `npm run build` confirms zero `fight-wear` PDP routes generated (`dynamicParams = false` + `generateStaticParams` filters to `"published"`, and there are none), the PLP itself statically generated and its own URL the only `fight-wear` entry in `sitemap.xml`. Live: PLP title tag renders `Custom BJJ Rash Guard Manufacturer | Capriowear` (brand-name rule confirmed — the root layout's `title.template` appends `| Capriowear` automatically, never `| Caprio Sports`; "Caprio Sports" appears only inside the entity FAQ's appended `companyIdentity` line, from `content/site.ts`, same as every other category). PLP JSON-LD is exactly `BreadcrumbList` + `FAQPage` (+ sitewide `Organization`/`WebSite`) — `CollectionPage`/`ItemList` correctly omitted with zero published styles. Both hero PDP URLs (`/teamwear/fight-wear/long-sleeve-rash-guard`, `/teamwear/fight-wear/fight-shorts`) 404 live, confirming the draft gate holds.

**Two prose deviations found and flagged, not fixed** (would require editing `categoryEntityFaq()` in `content/activewear/pdpShared.ts`, shared verbatim by every Activewear and Teamwear category — 27+ other categories — so not something to change unilaterally on this one category's say-so): (1) `manufacturerNoun: "Rash Guard & Fight Wear"` lowercases to a literal `&` in the auto-built entity-FAQ sentence ("a custom rash guard & fight wear manufacturer"), where the brief's own given assembled answer reads "and" instead — the ampersand is otherwise correct and intentional for `h1`/`menuLabel` display use, just reads oddly inline in prose; (2) the generator's own audience clause is a fixed "for activewear brands and teamwear suppliers worldwide" for every category, where this brief's own given answer asks for "for combat-sports brands, academies and teamwear suppliers worldwide" — the audience clause has never been made a per-category override (unlike `manufacturerNoun`/`productNounPlural`/`entityExampleStyles`/`entityFabrics`, each added for exactly this kind of gap). Both are narrow, sentence-level wording gaps, not hard-rule violations — flagged for the owner to decide whether a fifth `categoryEntityFaq()` override field is worth adding, same pattern as every prior gap of this kind.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` all clean. Nothing committed — no file changed.

### 5ef · Sitewide SEO/metadata plumbing audit: noindex switch added, SITE_URL made env-overridable — 2026-09-06
Owner: full-site audit of metadata/schema/robots/config wiring (no content or layout changes). Result, 8 items:

1. **Indexing switch — fixed.** Added `NEXT_PUBLIC_ALLOW_INDEXING` (defaults OFF), read into `content/site.ts`'s new `ALLOW_INDEXING` constant. `app/layout.tsx` now sets `robots: {index: false, follow: false}` on the sitewide `Metadata` object while off (Next's own Metadata API, not a hand-written `<meta>` tag — same rendered output); `app/robots.ts` returns a blanket `Disallow: /` with no sitemap reference while off, reverting to its exact prior behavior (styleguide disallowed, everything else allowed, sitemap referenced) once flipped on. One var flips both. See docs/06-seo.md's new "Indexing switch" section.
2. **Production base URL — fixed.** `SITE_URL` now reads `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`, falling back to the exact prior literal (`https://www.capriosports.com/capriowear`) when unset — every canonical/sitemap/OG call site already reads this one constant, so nothing else needed to change.
3. **Sitemap draft exclusion — found correct, no fix needed.** `app/sitemap.ts` already filters every PDP enumeration to `status === "published"` (Activewear and Teamwear alike). Confirmed post-build: 34 total sitemap URLs, zero draft slugs present (spot-checked 3 known drafts).
4. **Draft routes — found correct, no fix needed.** Both `[category]/[style]` and `[sport]/[style]` set `dynamicParams = false` and filter `generateStaticParams` to `"published"` — confirmed post-build that no route directory exists for any draft style; a direct hit 404s.
5. **Per-page canonical/OG — found correct, no fix needed.** Every PLP/PDP `generateMetadata` builds its own `canonical` from `SITE_URL` + that page's own slug/href, and its own OG title/description from that page's own content fields. PDP OG image already omits `openGraph.images` entirely when no real photo `src` exists yet (falls through to the route's own `opengraph-image.tsx` file-convention default, not the sitewide `DEFAULT_OG_IMAGE` constant — a prior audit, 2026-09-02, already removed that hardcoded fallback).
6. **Image alt text — found correct, no fix needed.** `StyleCard.imageAlt`/`images[].alt` already exist and are required per entry; `ProductCardMedia.tsx`/`ProductGallery.tsx` both render real `<Image alt={...}>` with a sensible label fallback, confirmed in the component source.
7. **basePath — flagged, not fixed.** `next.config.ts` sets no `basePath`; every internal route/link in this app starts at `/`, while `SITE_URL` is manually prefixed with `/capriowear` for every absolute canonical/OG/sitemap URL only. This is the owner-confirmed architecture (2026-08-25, `content/site.ts`'s own comment): the `/capriowear` mount is handled by something outside this repo (a reverse proxy/rewrite in front of `www.capriosports.com`), not Next's own `basePath` feature. Flagging rather than guessing: if that outside layer ever strips the `/capriowear` prefix before forwarding rather than rewriting transparently, this app's own root-relative internal links (`href="/activewear/..."`) would resolve wrong once actually live under the subpath — verifying that proxy behavior is outside this repo's own tools and wasn't attempted here.
8. **Schema sanity — found correct, no fix needed.** Confirmed across both Activewear and Teamwear routes: `BreadcrumbList` on every PLP/PDP; `FAQPage` only alongside a visibly rendered `Faq`; `Product` (`lib/schema.ts`'s `productSchema()`, never a price field) only when `status === "published"` (draft PDPs return `{}`/`notFound()` before the schema call is ever reached); `CollectionPage`/`ItemList` gated on `publishedStyleCards.length > 0` on every PLP (Activewear and Teamwear alike).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` all clean. Live: sitewide `<meta name="robots">` reads `noindex, nofollow`; `/robots.txt` reads `User-Agent: *` / `Disallow: /` with no sitemap line, both confirmed in dev and in the production build output. Site currently NOT indexable — confirmed the default (unset env var) state, the state this ships in.

### 5eg · PDP top section: tablet-width (768–1279px) side-by-side layout reverted, main image was too small — 2026-09-07
Owner: "The PDP top section, above the specifications should follow the mobile layout, not desktop as the main product image size is very small." This reverses one piece of 5ds (2026-09-04's homepage-wide tablet treatment, applied to the PDP): that pass had moved the gallery+info row's breakpoint from `xl:` (1280px) to `md:` (768px), which forced `ProductGallery`'s desktop side-by-side layout (109px thumbnail rail + main image) into the 768–1279px tablet range at a judgment-call `md:w-[320px]` column width (no tablet Figma frame exists) — leaving only ~195px for the actual main image, confirmed too small live, exactly matching the owner's report.

Reverted the `md:`→`xl:` breakpoint on every class 5ds touched for this section, in both PDP page files (`app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx`, `app/teamwear/[sport]/[style]/page.tsx`) and two recipes:
- The gallery+info row wrapper: `md:flex-row md:items-start md:gap-8 md:pt-6` → `xl:flex-row xl:items-start xl:gap-[66px] xl:pt-6` (dropped the now-redundant `xl:gap-8` the row also carried, since `xl:gap-[66px]` already won that cascade).
- The text column: dropped the now-fully-redundant `md:min-w-0 md:flex-1` pair entirely (nothing left to override below `xl` once the fixed `xl:w-[514px] xl:flex-none` is the only sized tier) — `w-full` at every width under `xl`, matching the mobile layout it now shares through tablet.
- `productGallery.desktopRoot`: `md:sticky md:top-[24px] md:flex md:w-[320px] md:shrink-0 md:gap-4 md:self-start xl:w-[700px]` → `xl:sticky xl:top-[24px] xl:flex xl:w-[700px] xl:shrink-0 xl:gap-4 xl:self-start` (the tablet-specific `320px` cap dropped entirely, not just relabeled).
- `productGallery.mobileRoot`: `md:-mx-8 md:hidden` → `xl:-mx-8 xl:hidden` — full-width stacked image now covers the whole 768–1279px range.
- `productCtas.desktopRow`/`mobileBar`: `md:flex md:mt-2` / `md:hidden` → `xl:flex xl:mt-2` / `xl:hidden`, so the CTA row switches in step with the gallery rather than a breakpoint earlier on its own.

Deliberately left unchanged: `productCtas.mobileButton`'s own `md:` tablet-width button-cap fix (2026-09-03) — that fix stops the sticky bar's button from stretching full-bleed at tablet width, which is newly relevant again now that the bar itself renders all the way through 1279px, not superseded by this revert. `ProductInfo.heading`'s `md:text-2xl` size bump — pure typography, unrelated to the layout squeeze the owner flagged, and larger heading text at tablet width is desirable regardless of which gallery layout shows. `ProductInfo.tsx`/`ProductHighlights.tsx`/`ProductOptions.tsx` — none carry any `md:` breakpoint, already width-agnostic.

**A second, complementary fix found already in progress on the same file from a concurrent session**: `ProductRelatedStyles`' two page-level instances (desktop position inside the text column, mobile/tablet position after `TrustPoints`) had independently drifted to `md:flex`/`md:hidden` in 5ds and were already being corrected back to `xl:flex`/`xl:hidden` on the Activewear PDP template when this task started (owner, on that section's own spacing: "make it 72px only on both mobile and tablet") — left as-is, and the identical fix was mirrored to the Teamwear PDP template here, which hadn't been updated yet.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 768px, 1024px, and 1279px: full-width stacked mobile layout renders (image + thumbnail strip, sticky CTA bar), matching real mobile's own layout exactly; the desktop side-by-side layout (700px gallery, 514px text column, inline CTA row) only appears from 1280px up; `xl:` desktop (1440px) confirmed pixel-identical to before. `npx playwright test tests/screenshots.spec.ts` (30 tests, via a local temp Playwright config to route around this worktree's known duplicate-`@playwright/test`-module issue) all green.

### 5eh · PDP tablet breadcrumb: gap below now matches the gap above — 2026-09-07
Same-day follow-up to 5eg. Owner: "Breadcrumbs on the tablet pdp, should have the same gap to the image as it has from the top." `breadcrumb.nav` (`"container-p pb-2 pt-8"`) is unconditional, so the gap above the breadcrumb (header → breadcrumb) was already a flat 32px at every width it's visible (`hidden md:block`, i.e. `md:` up). The gap below it (breadcrumb → gallery image) came from the gallery/info row wrapper's own top padding — `pt-0` by default, only `xl:pt-6` (24px) added it back — so at tablet width the row still had `pt-0` (no `md:` override existed), leaving just `breadcrumb.nav`'s own `pb-2` (8px) below it: a real 32px/8px mismatch, exactly the owner's report, unaffected by 5eg's own gallery/CTA breakpoint reverts (this gap is governed by `pt-0`/`xl:pt-6`, not the `md:`↔`xl:` values 5eg touched).

Added `md:pt-6` to the row wrapper in both `app/activewear/[category]/[style]/page.tsx` (~line 269) and `app/teamwear/[sport]/[style]/page.tsx` (~line 164): `"container-p flex flex-col gap-6 pt-0 xl:flex-row xl:items-start xl:gap-[66px] xl:pt-6"` → `"...pt-0 md:pt-6 xl:flex-row xl:items-start xl:gap-[66px] xl:pt-6"`. `xl:pt-6` is now redundant with the new `md:pt-6` (same 24px value) but was kept rather than collapsed, matching this project's existing pattern elsewhere of layering breakpoint values instead of removing an equal one once a lower tier catches up. Not touched: `breadcrumb.nav`'s own `pt-8`/`pb-2` (correct and unconditional already) or its `hidden md:block` visibility toggle.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression`: gap above the breadcrumb (header→breadcrumb) and gap below it (breadcrumb→image) both measure 32px at 768px, 1024px, and 1279px (was 32px/8px before); unchanged 32px/32px at 1440px `xl:` desktop; unchanged 0px gap (breadcrumb hidden, image directly under header) at real mobile (375px). No published Teamwear PDP style exists yet to spot-check live (all current teamwear styles are drafts), but the page file's own wrapper class was changed identically to the already-verified Activewear template. `npx playwright test tests/screenshots.spec.ts` (30 tests, same local-config workaround as 5eg) all green.

### 5ei · PDP tablet: three sections given a real, self-owned 120px top gap; Related Styles rebalanced to a clean 48px/48px — 2026-09-07
Same-day follow-up, prompted by a status question ("what's the gap from top for sections: specifications, how we customize, and built to pass the squat text?" on the leggings PDP) that surfaced the inconsistency: at tablet width (768–1279px), `Specifications` had 72px, `HOW WE CUSTOMIZE` had 120px, and `TrustPoints` ("Built to pass the squat test") had 0px of its own — the 200px visual gap above `TrustPoints` came entirely from `ProductCustomizeSteps`' own bottom padding, not from `TrustPoints` itself, a "gap borrowed from whoever happens to have padding" pattern already present in several places on this page. Owner: "for PDP tablet, follow 120px from top of each section, 0px from the bottom of the section. for these sections only 'Specifications', 'HOW WE CUSTOMIZE', and 'Built to pass the squat test'."

Four `components/ui/styles.ts` recipe edits, `md:` tier only — real mobile and `xl:` desktop untouched in every case:
- `productSpecifications.section`: `pt-[72px] xl:pt-[120px] xl:pb-[40px]` → `pt-[72px] md:pt-[120px] xl:pb-[40px]` — `md:pt-[120px]` cascades the already-correct desktop value down to tablet (the now-redundant separate `xl:` tier dropped). Bottom was already 0 at tablet, untouched.
- `ProductCustomizeSteps`' `desktopOuter`: `pt-[120px] pb-[200px]` → `pt-[120px] pb-0 xl:pb-[200px]` (both on the `hidden md:flex...` wrapper) — top was already 120px at tablet (this div only ever renders from `md:` up), no change; bottom splits into a new tablet value (0) and an explicit desktop restore.
- `trustPoints.sidePaddingPdp`: `pb-12 md:pb-[72px] xl:px-[80px] xl:pb-[120px]` → `pb-12 md:pb-0 md:pt-[120px] xl:pt-0 xl:px-[80px] xl:pb-[120px]` — this section gains a real top gap of its own at tablet for the first time (previously it had none at all, on any width, relying entirely on `ProductCustomizeSteps`' bottom); `xl:pt-0` explicitly cancels that cascade at desktop, which still relies on `ProductCustomizeSteps`' own `xl:pb-[200px]`, unchanged.

**A real side effect, caught before implementing**: zeroing `TrustPoints`' tablet bottom would collapse its gap to the next section, `ProductRelatedStyles`' mobile/tablet instance (`topRuleNone: pt-0 pb-12 md:pb-[72px]`), which also owned no top gap of its own and was passively relying on `TrustPoints`' bottom the same way — asked the owner before proceeding; answer: "add 48px top gap for related style and remove any other gaps it has it must be 48px from the top and bottom only." Fourth edit: `productRelatedStyles.topRuleNone`: `pt-0 pb-12 md:pb-[72px]` → `pt-0 md:pt-12 pb-12` — `md:pt-12` (48px) gives this instance a real top gap of its own at tablet; the old `md:pb-[72px]` tablet-only override is dropped entirely, since the unprefixed `pb-12` (48px) already covers tablet mobile-first once nothing overrides it — replacing the old asymmetric 48px-top(borrowed)/72px-bottom split with a clean, self-owned 48px/48px. This instance only ever renders below `xl:` (`xl:hidden` at its call site), so the desktop-only sibling instance (`topRuleDefault`) is untouched.

Resulting tablet gaps: gallery/info row → Specifications 120px; Specifications → HOW WE CUSTOMIZE 120px; HOW WE CUSTOMIZE → TrustPoints 120px; TrustPoints → Related Styles 48px; Related Styles' own bottom 48px.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings/high-waisted-compression` at 768px/1024px/1279px: all four gaps confirmed 120/120/120/48, Related Styles' own bottom confirmed 48px. `xl:` desktop (1440px) confirmed pixel-identical to before at every boundary (Specifications 120/40, ProductCustomizeSteps 120/200, TrustPoints 0/120 — still relying on ProductCustomizeSteps). Real mobile (375px) confirmed pixel-identical to before (Specifications 72/0, TrustPoints 0/48). `npx playwright test tests/screenshots.spec.ts` (30 tests, same local-config workaround as 5eg/5eh) all green.

### 5el · Sitewide text-selection colour changed from brand orange to neutral gray — 2026-09-07
Owner: "When I select a text on the web, it highlights it as orange, make it neutral color, not brand color." `app/globals.css`'s one sitewide `::selection` rule was `background: var(--color-accent); color: var(--color-accent-ink)` — the brand orange with white text, applying everywhere (no per-section variant). Changed to `background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-muted) 40%, transparent); color: inherit` — `--color-muted` (`#6B6B74`, the project's existing secondary-text gray token) as a translucent overlay reads correctly on both light (near-black text) and dark (white text) sections without needing two rules, and `color: inherit` lets whichever text colour is already there show through instead of forcing a fixed one that would only work on one of the two contexts.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live: selected a heading on a dark section via the Selection API (`window.getSelection()`) and screenshotted it — a neutral translucent gray highlight, text still legible, no orange. `npx playwright test tests/screenshots.spec.ts` (30 tests) all green (this change has no layout effect, only a pseudo-element style).

### 5eo · Homepage "What We Make", real mobile: capped to 4 tiles per group + a "View All" CTA — 2026-09-07
Owner: "On the mobile homepage, add a cta after 4 products in what we make section 1 cta under activewear after 4 products, and cta for teamwear, it will take customers to their respective landing pages. Show the first 2 sub-cat only. Decide the cta, sta[rt] space from the top should be 40px." Clarified via follow-up: 4 tiles (not 2), CTA copy "View All Activewear"/"View All Teamwear & Uniforms." `content/home.ts`'s `whatWeMake.categories` already carried a `href` per category (`/activewear`, `/teamwear`, added earlier, unused) — no content-shape change needed.

Because a single CSS-reflowed tile list can't have a different tile *count* per breakpoint (only a different layout — `mobileList` already became a real 2-column grid from `md:` up, see 5ds-era entries), the real-mobile list split into two real DOM instances in `WhatWeMake.tsx`, the same "two breakpoint-specific instances" pattern already used elsewhere (e.g. `ProductGallery`): `whatWeMake.mobileListCapped` (`flex flex-col gap-10 md:hidden`, sliced to `MOBILE_TILE_LIMIT = 4`) for real mobile plus the new CTA; `whatWeMake.mobileListFull` (the old `mobileList`, renamed, `hidden md:grid md:grid-cols-2 md:gap-x-6 md:gap-y-10 xl:hidden`) for tablet, every tile, no CTA, unchanged. Desktop's `Card` grid (`desktopGrid`) is untouched either way.

**Three same-day corrections, each caught live before landing**:
1. Teamwear's CTA label shortened to "View All Teamwear" (owner: "for teamwear just use view all teamwear") — the group's own `<h3>` keeps "Teamwear & Uniforms," only the CTA label differs, via a small `isTeamwear` check in `WhatWeMake.tsx`.
2. Both CTAs' outline/text colour moved from `button.secondary`'s default `currentColor` to the brand accent orange — first Teamwear only ("make the outline and text orange as primary color"), then extended to Activewear the same day ("make view all activewear also orange outline with text"). `whatWeMake.mobileGroupCta` gained `!border-accent !text-accent hover:!bg-[#FFF6F3]` (`!` needed for the same same-specificity reason already documented on `productCtas.secondaryDesktop`, whose exact hover tint this reuses).
3. Top spacing: first "40px," corrected same day to "32px." A first pass used `mt-8` (32px) directly on the button — live measurement showed 56px, not 32, because `whatWeMake.group`'s own `gap-6` (24px) flex gap already applies between every child in that column; a margin on a flex child adds to that shared gap rather than replacing it (the exact mechanism `productCtas.desktopRow`'s own `xl:mt-2` already documents: "32 + 8 = a real 40px"). Corrected to `mt-2` (8px): `8 + 24 = 32px`, confirmed live.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean (a full `eslint .` run also surfaced unrelated noise from a stray `.next/build` directory inside a concurrent session's own worktree — confirmed pre-existing and unrelated by linting the two touched files directly, both clean). Live at 375px: both groups show exactly 4 tiles then an orange-outlined "View All" CTA, 32px below the last tile, linking to `/activewear`/`/teamwear`. At 1024px (tablet): both groups show every tile (7 Activewear, 4 Teamwear) in the unchanged 2-column grid, no CTA. At 1440px (desktop): `Card` grid unchanged, 7 tiles. Full 30-viewport screenshot suite passing.

### 5ep · Product Range: confirmed the title reveal, fixed a real dark-orange hover bug — 2026-09-07
Owner: "add the title animation to end-to-end title and on hover on the explore cta make it light orange not dark."

The heading already had the word-by-word reveal (`<TextReveal as="span" text={content.h2} />`) from this section's first build, same pattern as the eyebrow and every other section's heading — confirmed via SSR markup rather than re-adding anything.

**A real bug in the "Explore" link's hover state**: `exploreLink` used this codebase's own sitewide default hover treatment, `hover:opacity-80`, which dims a colour toward the page background. Every other hover link on the site sits on a light/paper background, where dimming reads as lighter — but this link sits on `bg-ink` (near-black), so the same mechanism mixed the orange toward black instead, reading as a darker orange, backwards from the request. Fixed with `hover:brightness-125`, which lightens the actual colour via a CSS filter rather than blending it toward whatever's behind it — no new colour token needed, and the filter also lightens `NextArrowIcon` for free (it inherits `currentColor` from the same `<Link>`).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .`, `npm run build` all clean. Confirmed via SSR output: `reveal-word` spans with per-word `--reveal-index` render for "End-to-end activewear and teamwear manufacturing"; both "Explore" links carry `hover:brightness-125`, not the old `hover:opacity-80`.

### 5eq · Explore chevron: same hover nudge as PDP Related Styles — 2026-09-07
Owner: "on the hover chvron should have the same animation we applied on pdp, related styles." `NextArrowIcon` gained the exact hover treatment PDP's Related Styles chip chevron already established (`productRelatedStyles.chipIcon`'s `group-hover:translate-x-0.5`, a small right-nudge), not a new one-off animation: `exploreLink` gained `group`, `exploreIcon` gained `transition-transform group-hover:translate-x-0.5`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Confirmed via SSR output: both "Explore" links' chevrons carry `group-hover:translate-x-0.5`.
